## Voice and place reminder screens (2026-10-10)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 217 | **Voice and place reminder screens designed and approved** (owner: "approved, build all 5 changes"; Doddy Notifications canvas, screens 7–14). The voice card shows the note with its saved date, lets the user change the time or switch arrive/leave before Set reminder, and is followed by an on-screen receipt (the note screen's `NoteReminderReceiptView`, with Try again on a user tap); Open the note only after the conversation ended. A place reminder blocked by a setting offers "Remind me at a time instead". | Accepted; app compile and device NOT RUN |

## Repeating reminders and snooze (2026-10-10)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 218 | **Repeating note reminders and more snooze** (owner: "approved, build it" for canvas screens 15–21; decisions R1–R4 as proposed). Time reminders can repeat every day, on weekdays, every week or every month (days 1–28 only); a repeat starts at its next matching time and fires at the same clock time in the zone it was set in. One repeating iOS request per rule, five for weekdays; the 50 limit counts iOS slots. Done on a repeat means this time only (`last_done_at`); Stop repeating is on the note only, after a question. Lock Screen: one-off Open note / In 1 hour / Tomorrow 09:00 / Done; repeating Open note / In 1 hour / Done this time, title "Reminder · every Monday" (never the note). Home's Later: In 1 hour, This evening (before 17:00), Tomorrow morning, Pick a time. A snoozed one-off moves (remembering the time first set); a snoozed repeat gets one extra request and carries on; a refused snooze leaves the reminder unchanged. Voice: `remind_me_about_note` gains `repeat`. Migration `v5_note_reminder_repeat` (new columns with CHECKs). | Accepted; app compile and device NOT RUN |

ADR-218 was first recorded here as ADR-200; renumbered 2026-10-10 because ADR-200 to ADR-204 are used on the `claude/loving-dirac-uuxoux` line (ADR-200 there: type-check of the non-UI app files).

## Languages and translator (2026-10-10)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 212 | **All European languages, switching freely** (owner: "make sure doddy can talk with all european language and change between them freely"). The live agent gets 36 language presets plus English, each overriding only the greeting. ElevenLabs refused Basque and Albanian. The existing `language_detection` tool then switches mid-conversation. The prompt says to follow the user's language; the Wikipedia tools take the same 37 codes, with summary redirects limited to the 20 largest editions. Settings "Starts in" offers Automatic plus the 25 languages the ElevenLabs Swift SDK can start in; stored values are unchanged. The `propose_plan` drift from ADR-204 (other line) was kept on the live agent. Details: `docs/implementation/LANGUAGES_STATUS.md`. | Accepted; live agent updated; no live conversation tested in the new languages |
| 213 | **Polish notification text** (owner chose "1."). When the iPhone's first language is Polish, the Lock Screen title, private body, repeat wording and long-press buttons are Polish (`NotificationLanguage`, `NoteReminderCopy`). Other languages stay English until reviewed. The note text is never translated. Doddy rewrites waiting notifications after a language change. In-app screens stay English: the app has no localisation set up yet, which is a separate step. | Accepted; Polish copy needs native review (D6) |
| 214 | **Translator mode: design proposal** (owner: "design translator mode … suggest design"). Canvas "Doddy Translator" and `docs/design/TRANSLATOR_MODE.md`. Face to face with the other person's half rotated; AI disclosure to them first, in their language; a separate tool-less agent; translate, never answer; exact cards for numbers; careful mode for health, law, police and money; nothing kept unless tapped within 5 minutes. Decisions T1–T6 are for the owner. | Proposed; not built |
| 215 | **Translator mode: first build** (owner: "build translator mode"; decisions T1–T5 as answered under ADR-214). A mode of the main conversation: new client tools `start_translator` (shown as a "Start translating?" card, switches only on Start) and `stop_translator`. While it is on the app refuses every other tool call before it is logged or run (`TranslatorPolicy`), the conversation is private and locked private, and transcript lines go to an in-memory `TranslatorSession`, never to the conversation log. Face to face (their half rotated 180°) and listen only. Each of Doddy's lines goes to a half by its own language, then by the language of what was said, then by the held hold-to-talk side, then by taking turns. "Doddy, …" is a quiet word on the owner's half. Exact facts (prices, times, phone numbers, addresses, numbers with units) are extracted in code; careful mode turns on from a multilingual word list and stays on. The end screen keeps the text in memory for 5 minutes for Copy or "Save the facts as a note" (a normal explicit save). Home › Translate starts a conversation with no notes, recent conversations or profile sent (`{{translator_start}}`). The AI disclosure, AI label, hold-to-talk and careful lines exist for all 37 languages, unreviewed. T6 native voices deferred to a listening pass: Doddy's own voice for now. See `docs/implementation/TRANSLATOR_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; no live conversation run |
| 216 | **Lists by voice** (owner: "design lists by voice", then "approved, build it": L1–L6 as proposed). A list is a kind of note: a `memories` row with category `list` (its text is the name) plus `list_items` (migration `v6_list_items`, deleted with the list). Memories › Lists; a list screen with ticks, Done, Clear ticked, Untick all, Share as text, Rename, Delete (with Undo) and note reminders. By voice: `add_to_list`, `read_list`, `check_off`, `remove_from_list` change at once on the user's request (L1) with a verified receipt and Undo; `create_list` is a card (L2). The app matches lists and items exactly and asks on none or several; recall never returns lists. Ticked items stay until cleared (L3); Doddy-only lists (L4); no starter lists (L5); list names and counts go to the agent at the start, items only when read or changed (L6). Canvas "Doddy Lists", `docs/design/LISTS.md`, `docs/implementation/LISTS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; no live conversation run |
| 217 | **List follow-ups** (owner: "do all of them"). Find shows lists whose name or items match the search words (`ListSearch`), apart from the notes. A JSON export restore puts list items back into their lists (`MemoryRestore.listItems`); items already here or whose list is gone are skipped. A list's private reminder says "A list you asked to see again." (Polish: "Lista, którą chcesz zobaczyć ponownie."). A plan card can hold `add_to_list` steps, matched exactly when they run, not changeable on the card. The button still says "Open note". | Built; not compiled with Xcode |
| 218 | **Photo notes** (owner: "do all of them"). A note can carry one photo (`memory_photos`, migration `v7_memory_photos`, deleted with the note). Picked with Apple's photo picker only: no camera and no photo library permission. Downscaled to 2048 px JPEG and kept in a `NotePhotos` folder next to the notes database (Data Protection until first unlock, in iPhone backups like the database). Its text is read on this iPhone with Vision (no language correction, cut at 3,000 characters on a whole line) and added to the editor after the user's own words, with a notice to check it; the saved words are what the user accepted, source stays `explicit_text`. The file is written and read back before the link; a photo that can't be kept after the note saved is said plainly. Delete keeps the file through Undo; Remove photo keeps the words. Photos are never sent to ElevenLabs or a server and aren't in exports. Capability guard: Vision and the picker only in `DoddyApp/Features/Photos`; camera and library APIs refused. Camera capture is an owner decision (needs `NSCameraUsageDescription`). `docs/implementation/PHOTO_NOTES_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; text reading not run on a device |
| 219 | **Home and Lock Screen widgets** (owner: "do all of them"; "Yes, tick from widget"). Four widgets: Pinned notes, Next reminder (time reminders only), List (choose one; tap an item to tick or untick it) and Talk to Doddy. Widgets never open the database: the app writes a small snapshot (`WidgetSnapshot`: up to 4 pinned notes, 3 next reminders, 8 lists × 12 items, first line cut at 120 characters) to an App Group file, reads it back, and reloads the widgets after every change. Reminder text follows the notification setting (hidden by default). Note text is `privacySensitive`, so iOS hides it on the Lock Screen while locked. A tick runs `ToggleListItemIntent`, a `LiveActivityIntent` performed in the app's process like ADR-131: the store re-reads the list, changes the item only if the widget showed it as it is now, and the change is read back; the list screen's Undo covers it. Ticking needs the iPhone unlocked. Settings › Widgets › "Show notes and lists in widgets" (on) writes an empty snapshot when off. App Group is off by default (`DODDY_APP_ENTITLEMENTS`, `DODDY_WIDGET_ENTITLEMENTS`); without it widgets say "Open Doddy to set up widgets". `doddy://open/<id>` opens a note or list. `docs/implementation/WIDGETS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; widgets never drawn |
| 220 | **Morning check-in** (owner: "do all of them"). Off by default. Settings › Morning check-in: time (08:00) and Weekdays only. A repeating local notification (1, or 5 on weekdays; read back from iOS) says only "Good morning · A quick look at your day?" (Polish when the iPhone is in Polish). A tap opens the check-in screen, built on this iPhone: today's Doddy time reminders still ahead, lists with things left, and the Today card (Calendar and Reminders, when connected). Nothing is sent until "Talk it through", which opens a conversation with `{{morning_checkin}}` (`MorningCheckInPrompt`); after Doddy's greeting the app asks for a short briefing, and the agent may call `read_calendar` for today under the usual Calendar switch. Not offered in a translator start. "What leaves this iPhone" says so while it is on. `docs/implementation/MORNING_CHECKIN_STATUS.md`. | Built; live agent updated; not compiled with Xcode; no live conversation run |
| 221 | **Translator follow-ups** (owner: "do all of them"). Listen only gets a "Read aloud" switch: off sets the agent's audio track to volume 0 on this iPhone (LiveKit `RemoteAudioTrack.volume`, re-applied when Doddy speaks, read back; a problem line otherwise), restored when the mode ends. A native-review sheet of the 36 non-English translator lines is generated from `TranslatorLanguages.all` (`TranslatorReviewSheet`, guarded by a test). Native voices (T6): a 15-voice shortlist for Polish, German, Spanish, French and Italian, unheard, and the constraint that one conversation has one voice; no voice changed. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; read-aloud off not heard on a device |
| 222 | **Encrypted backup file** (owner: "A file you keep"). Settings › Encrypted backup. One `.doddybackup` file: the notes and lists exactly as `MemoryExport.json`, photos (optional), time reminders (next occurrence; place reminders stay on their iPhone) and About you answers, as JSON sealed with AES-256-GCM. The key comes from the passphrase (NFC, at least 10 characters) with PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 600,000 rounds and a random 16-byte salt. The header is authenticated; files asking for under 100,000 rounds are refused. Each backup is opened again and compared before the save sheet appears; "Saved as …" only on the sheet's success. Restore only adds: `BackupRestorePlan` takes notes not here, their list items, photos and reminders, and the profile only into an empty one; each part is counted only when read back. No server, no account, no passphrase kept. CryptoKit on iPhone; swift-crypto 3.15.1 on Linux only, for tests (Linux test link needs `-Xlinker --allow-shlib-undefined`). `docs/implementation/ENCRYPTED_BACKUP_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; no file made on a device |
| 223 | **App screens in Polish** (owner: "do all of them"). A String Catalog (`DoddyApp/Resources/Localizable.xcstrings`) generated by `tools/build_strings_catalog.py` from the reviewed table `l10n/pl.json` (English source text → Polish). SwiftUI looks up `Text`, `Button`, `Label`, `Toggle`, `Section` and similar literals, and `JourneyHeading` now looks its strings up too; `CFBundleLocalizations` is en, pl, so iOS shows Polish when the iPhone is in Polish. 562 of 606 literal UI strings are translated (the opening's strings added at the second join) (informal "Ty", Doddy as "on"); the longest privacy paragraphs, developer-only screens and every string built at run time (with values in it, or from Swift code such as error messages) stay English. `--check` in CI fails when the catalog is stale or a Polish line's English string has left the app. Not reviewed by a second Polish speaker. `docs/implementation/POLISH_SCREENS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never seen on a Polish iPhone |
| 224 | **Owner answers on this round's open questions** (2026-10-10). (1) Camera capture for photo notes: **no**; photo notes stay picker-only and the capability guard keeps refusing camera APIs. (2) Translator voice: **Doddy's own voice** for both sides, with each language spoken natively and written with correct spelling and accents; the translator prompt now says so; no native-voice switching (the shortlist stays for reference only). (3) Widgets show notes and lists **by default** (as built). (4) Moving the notes database into the App Group: **not now**; tick-from-widget stays on the in-app intent until a device test says otherwise. (5) Personality and voice settings in the backup file: **undecided**; left out as built. | Recorded; prompt line applied to the live agent |

ADR-223 pack check: `schemas/memory.schema.json` gains the category `list` (exports now carry list records), so its hash in `PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json` was updated, as with ADR-107.

ADR-231 pack check: `schemas/memory.schema.json` gains the source `imported_file` (exports now carry imported notes), so its hash in `PACKAGE_MANIFEST.json` was updated, as with ADR-107 and ADR-216.

ADR-214 owner answers (2026-10-10, "1. both 2 mode 3. what 4 yes 5. yes 6 native"): T1 face to face and listen only; T2 a mode of Doddy (no separate agent); T4 detect by language plus hold-to-talk; T5 quiet word yes; T6 a native voice per language. T3: nothing kept, copy or save the facts within 5 minutes on a tap.

Lines joined again 2026-10-10: the lists line (PR #20, ADR-210 to ADR-224) was merged with `claude/loving-dirac-uuxoux` after PR #16. Both had used ADR-210, so the opening decision below was renumbered 210 → 225 (19 references in code and docs); commit messages before this merge keep the old number.

## The opening and listening on open (2026-10-10, owner: "a very cool app opening every time … he scores … then the normal UI … turn it off in settings … already listening")

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 225 | **The opening and "Start listening when I open Doddy"** (`docs/implementation/OPENING_SCENE_STATUS.md`; redesigned the same day after the owner asked for it to be "more cartoony … quick animation drawing Doddy, he shoots, then a new version that is ready to speak shows up with the other buttons and you can interact"). Each time Doddy is opened, a 2.4-second cartoon over the ready app (timing and hand-over revised the same day: "the animation must align with him when he finishes … and more this animation cooler"): a pen sketches Doddy in stroke by stroke, the ball pops into his hand and bounces twice, he crouches (squash), springs up (stretch) and hops into the shot, dust puffs where the ball and his feet hit the floor, the ball flies with a ghost trail into the ring, "SWISH!" bursts out with rays, confetti flies out of the ring, the camera shakes and the ring wobbles (one light haptic), he pops up in the listening pose, and then **jumps along an arc into his exact place on Home** while the court and paper fade and Home shows through, landing with a squash. Home measures where it draws him (`onGeometryChange`) and the opening's last frame draws him there with the same view (`CharacterArtImage`, shared with `UploadedCharacterView`), so when the opening goes there is no jump (`OpeningHandover`, tested: the last frame is exactly Home's box, unsquashed, never faded). Home's own picture is hidden while the opening plays. Talk to Doddy, Ask Doddy, Save and Find slide in as he jumps. A tap jumps straight to the hand-over instead of cutting. (1) The whole timeline is one pure function of time in DoddyCore (`OpeningScene.frame(at:)`, tested). (2) The four supplied v1.3-r1 basketball SVGs are bundled unchanged (`DoddyApp/Resources/Character/`) and read as vector strokes by `SVGPoseReader` (DoddyCore, tested against the real files: paths, circles, ellipses, nested translate/scale/rotate including rotation about a point, three colours by role; anything else is refused, so a changed drawing fails a test instead of drawing wrong). That is what lets the pen draw him and lets ink and paper follow dark appearance. The ball group is dropped and drawn by the scene. The ready pose is the app's own `UploadedDoddy_Listening`. No new character art was made; squash, stretch, hop and pop move whole drawings. No sound. (3) A tap skips to the hand-over; VoiceOver gets one element ("Doddy's opening … Double-tap to skip"). (4) Settings → Appearance → "Doddy's opening", on by default. With Reduce Motion it is a still picture: Doddy already in his place on Home, and the paper fades around him (1.1 s). With character artwork hidden, VoiceOver running, or a drawing missing from the bundle, it doesn't play. (5) What counts as opening the app (`OpeningPolicy`, tested): a launch with the app on screen, or a return after 5 minutes or more in the background; never over onboarding or another open screen, never from a background launch. (6) **No text greeting** (owner, same day: "without that 'hey it's Doddy'"): the speech bubble and Home's hello heading were removed, with `OpeningGreetingCopy`. Doddy's only greeting is the live agent's spoken first message (`voice-agent/session.json`) when a conversation starts. (7) **Listening on open**: Settings → Voice → "Start listening when I open Doddy", **on by default** (owner decision 2026-10-10: "turn listening on by default"; first shipped off, citing constitution DOD-11 and `README.md`'s no covert always-on mic). It is not covert: the conversation screen is up, the iOS indicator shows, and the switch turns it off. On, the opening ends by opening Talk to Doddy exactly as the button does (same controller, same iOS prompt, "Listening" only from real session events, the iOS indicator). It never opens when iOS has refused the microphone, when voice isn't available, when something else is on screen, or on a quick return. The usage string, the Privacy row and the switch's note say so. Nothing in the opening says "Listening". (8) UI tests launch with `-openingScene NO`; new tests cover play-and-skip, the automatic end, no replay on a quick return, off = Home at once, and the listening switch off by default. Browser preview of the same timeline: `design/opening-v0.1/` | Applied 2026-10-10. DoddyCore timeline, SVG reader, policy and greeting tested on Linux (Swift 6.1: 407 package tests pass, 36 of them for the opening); in the browser preview, the last frame and Home were compared pixel by pixel and match apart from image smoothing; `OpeningModel` passes the Linux compile check; the preview was rendered in headless Chromium. The SwiftUI scene, Home's ready state, the Settings rows and the UI tests are written but **not built or tried on a device** (no Xcode): Mac checklist §4b. The listening switch is on by default (owner decision); a fresh install sees the iOS microphone prompt the first time it opens after onboarding |

## Next round (2026-10-10, owner: "do all of them"; voice memos approved with "9 ok")

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 226 | **Lists from Siri and Shortcuts.** App Intents in the app target: Add to a list, Read a list, Tick an item, each with a `DoddyListEntity` (exact `ListMatch`, never a closest guess). They run in the app's process through the same store calls as voice and the list screen, and Siri says only what the store read back (`ListSiri`, Polish or English). Several items are split on commas, never on "and". A possibly-ambiguous tick ticks nothing. Unlocking is required, so no list is read out on a locked iPhone. Phrases name the list (`updateAppShortcutParameters()` when list names change). `docs/implementation/SIRI_LISTS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never asked of Siri |

| 227 | **Find by meaning.** Find shows "Close in meaning" under the word results: up to 5 saved notes (never lists or samples, never notes the word search already showed) whose vectors from Apple's on-device contextual embedding model are at least 0.5 similar and stand 0.08 above the average, labelled "Not the same words. Check it's the one you meant." Vectors live only in memory (`MeaningIndex`) and are dropped when a note changes or goes. The model may be downloaded from Apple once; no note or question text leaves the iPhone. Voice search is unchanged. Capability guard: NaturalLanguage only in `DoddyApp/Features/Meaning`. Thresholds need tuning on a device. `docs/implementation/MEANING_SEARCH_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; thresholds untuned |
| 228 | **Weekly tidy-up.** Off by default. Settings › Weekly tidy-up: day (Sunday) and time (18:00); one repeating notification, read back from iOS, saying only "Tidy up Doddy · A few minutes to tidy up?" (Polish on a Polish iPhone). The screen suggests notes saved twice with the same words (the first copy is kept; "Delete the copy from …" with confirmation and the usual Undo), one-off reminders more than a day past and never marked done ("Mark done", checked against the reloaded reminders), and lists with everything ticked ("Clear the ticked items" or "Delete the list"). "Keep them all" / "Leave it" hides a suggestion for this visit. Nothing changes without a tap; no streaks, counts or nagging. `docs/implementation/TIDY_UP_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode |
| 229 | **Move, rename and complete by voice.** Two phone tools: `change_calendar_event` (title and start exactly as `read_calendar` gave them, plus a new start, end or title) and `change_reminder` (exact title, plus a new due or `done: true`). The app finds exactly one match on the iPhone (same words and same minute, or same day for an all-day event; open reminders only), and otherwise tells the agent to ask. The card shows "Now" and "After". At the tap, the app checks again that the item still looks as shown, that the new time hasn't passed and that permission is still on. Then it writes and reads back before Doddy says "changed". A moved event keeps its length, an all-day event stays all-day, and a repeating event changes only this occurrence. An alarm set at the old due moves with it. Nothing is deleted. Paused by Pause all; logged in "What Doddy did". `docs/implementation/CALENDAR_CHANGES_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; live agent updated |
| 230 | **Voice memos.** Approved by the owner ("9 ok"). On Capture, "Record a voice memo" records up to 5 minutes (AAC, mono) to a temporary file; the microphone is on only between Record and Stop. Its words are written out on this iPhone by Apple's Speech framework with `requiresOnDeviceRecognition = true`. If the iPhone can't do that for the language, nothing is written out and nothing is sent. The text is added to the editor with "Speech-to-text makes mistakes, so check it before saving", and it can be written out again in another of the iPhone's languages. The note is saved first. Then the recording is copied into `NoteAudio` beside the database, read back, and linked (`memory_audio`, migration `v8_memory_audio`, read back). The note has play/pause and Remove recording. Deleting the note deletes its recording, kept while Undo is offered. Not in exports or backup files (backup contents unchanged, ADR-224), never sent to ElevenLabs or a server. New permission: speech recognition. Capability guard: Speech only in `Features/VoiceMemos`, every request on-device. `docs/implementation/VOICE_MEMOS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never recorded |
| 231 | **Import from Google Keep and text files.** Settings › Import notes. The user picks Google Takeout Keep `.json` files (unpacked in Files) or `.txt`/`.md` files; they are read once, on the iPhone. Every note is shown exactly as it will be saved, and nothing is saved until Import, only the ticked ones. A Keep note is its title, a blank line and its text, unchanged. A Keep checklist becomes a list with its ticked items ticked; an untitled checklist comes in as a note with one item per line (✓ for ticked) rather than a made-up list name. Keep photos, recordings and labels aren't imported, and the screen says so per note. Trashed notes, non-Keep JSON, non-UTF-8 files, files over 2 MB, notes over the length limit (never cut), and taken list names are listed with the reason. Notes already in Doddy start unticked. A text file is one note, or one per paragraph if the user asks. New source `imported_file` ("Imported from a file"), dated by Keep's creation time, otherwise "date unknown" (never the import time). Notes go in through `restore` (one transaction, read back). `docs/implementation/NOTE_IMPORT_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode |
| 232 | **Apple Watch app.** A watchOS 10 app (`DoddyWatch`, embedded in the iPhone app) with no database and no networking of its own. The iPhone sends it, via the WatchConnectivity application context, the same snapshot the widgets get: pinned notes, the next note reminders (their text only if notification text is on), and lists. It is under its own switch, Settings › Apple Watch › "Show notes and lists on Apple Watch" (on by default, like widgets, ADR-224). The watch can tick or untick a list item, and save a new note written or dictated and checked on the watch. Each is a message the iPhone answers from its store after reading the change back (same tick rule as the widgets: a stale tap changes nothing). The watch says "Saved" or "Ticked" only from that answer. If the iPhone isn't reachable, nothing is sent or queued. If a message gets no answer, the watch says it can't tell whether it happened. Capability guard: WatchConnectivity only in `DoddyApp/Features/Watch` and `DoddyWatch`; no GRDB, persistence or networking in the watch app. `docs/implementation/APPLE_WATCH_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never run on a watch |
| 233 | **Finishing Polish.** On-screen messages made in code use `NSLocalizedString` (247 of them; tool replies to the voice agent stay English). The catalog generator also writes the widget and watch catalogs, `InfoPlist.xcstrings` (the 6 permission prompts, from `l10n/pl_infoplist.json`, which fails the check when an English prompt changes) and `AppShortcuts.xcstrings` (19 Siri phrases, each naming the app once and keeping `${list}`). Coverage is 901 of 922 UI strings. The longest privacy paragraph waits for a native reviewer. `docs/implementation/POLISH_SCREENS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never seen on a Polish iPhone |
| 234 | **Owner answers on this round** (2026-10-11, "Yes add them , , yes"). (1) Voice memos go in the encrypted backup file as an option, "Include voice memos", on by default like photos. Restore attaches them only to notes it adds, read back, and older files without them still open. (2) The Apple Watch stays as built: content on by default. (3) A native speaker reviews the Polish before release, privacy texts and the AI disclosure first; until then the Polish is a draft. | Built (1); recorded (2, 3); not compiled with Xcode |

## Recall benchmark (2026-10-10, owner: "start with #1" from the DODDY AI agent research backlog, OP-016 + OP-008)

Numbering: these five were ADR-211 to ADR-215 on `claude/peaceful-hopper-v3gx75`, ADR-233 to ADR-237 after the first merge with the default branch, and ADR-249 to ADR-253 since the default branch took 233 and 234 (2026-10-11).

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 249 | **A fixed test set measures how well Doddy finds notes** (`docs/implementation/RECALL_BENCHMARK_STATUS.md`). The test set is `docs/quality/evals/recall_golden_v1.json`: 40 invented notes (20 English, 20 Polish) and 76 questions (40 English, 36 Polish). Every question names the notes that answer it, or none if nothing saved answers it. Every question is tagged with what it tests: exact words, Polish typed without diacritics, a different word ending, a question in the other language, a typo, spoken numbers, a different word for the same thing, an English 's, or nothing saved. `RecallBenchmarkTests` (DoddyPersistence tests) saves the notes into the real SQLite store and asks every question through `RepositoryRecallService` with the app's limit of 20. It then scores: a right note found, a right note first, in the top 3, MRR, wrong notes shown, and an honest "no match" when nothing answers. **Gate:** `recall_baseline_v1.json` records for each question whether it passed and whether a right note came first. The test fails when a question that passed or came first no longer does, so it runs in `scripts/ci.sh` like every package test. A planted regression was caught. **The baseline is raised only on purpose** (`scripts/recall_benchmark.sh --update-baseline`), together with the report `docs/quality/RECALL_BENCHMARK.md`. **No change to search** in this step. **Limits:** the labels were written by Claude, not by the owner or a native Polish speaker. The questions were written by someone who had read the matching rules, so they lean towards what the rules handle. The numbers are a regression signal for the word rules, not user accuracy. The research backlog's "human-labelled" requirement is not met until a person reviews the labels | Applied 2026-10-10. The test set and its 3 tests run on Linux (Swift 6.1.2). First result: right note found for 65 of 66 answerable questions, first for 61. Honest "no match" for **3 of 10** unanswerable questions. Two causes found and not yet fixed: the "s" of "What's" or "Jake's" becomes a search word that matches almost every note; short words match as prefixes ("car" finds "card") and as typos ("password" finds "passport"). Nothing in the app changed, so there is nothing to try on a device |
| 250 | **Fewer unrelated notes in search results** (owner: "fix causes 1 and 2" from ADR-249's first report). Three rule changes in `SearchFolding.queryTerms`, `RecallMatching` and `SQLiteMemoryRepository.query`. (1) **Single letters in a question are not searched.** The "s" of "What's" or "Jake's", or an initial like "witaminy D", prefix-matched almost every note. Single digits still count. (2) **A short word (3 letters or fewer) needs the whole word or a common ending.** The endings are English s/es and the common Polish case endings. So "car" finds "cars" but not "card", "kod" finds "kodu", and "lek" finds "leki" but not "lekarz". Digits keep the plain prefix ("four" still finds "4821"). (3) **A two-letter typo is only a fallback.** It counts for a question word only when none of the notes searched contain that word as typed. A one-letter typo of the word itself or of its fixed translation still always counts ("policy" finds Polish "polisy"). A typo against a shortened stem counts as two letters, because the stem is already one letter short. So "netflix password" no longer brings up the passport note, and "pasport" still finds it. Because all the notes searched are now judged together, the repository decides whether to scan beyond the index from the hits the rules accept, not from the raw index count. **How it was chosen:** the first version made every typo a fallback. The ADR-249 gate failed it, because "car insurance policy" lost its Polish answer from first place. The second version allowed one-letter typos through the stem, which a new unit test caught. The rules were tuned against the same AI-written test set, so the gain is not proof of real-world accuracy. **Trade-off:** a note that contains a misspelling ("pasword") is no longer found by the correct spelling when another note has the correct word. **Not changed:** one shared real word is still enough to list a note ("netflix password" lists both wifi passwords), so 5 of 10 unanswerable questions still list notes | Applied 2026-10-10. Recall benchmark on Linux (Swift 6.1.2): honest "no match" **3 → 5 of 10**; wrong notes shown per answerable question **2.5 → 1.3**; right note found (65/66) and first (61/66) unchanged; no question got worse. Baseline raised. 418 package tests pass (4 new). Search runs in DoddyCore, which the iOS app uses as it is; the app itself was not built (no Xcode) and nothing was tried on a device |
| 251 | **Say when the found notes answer only part of the question** (owner: "cause 3 should define it better and give better answer not ignore"). Recall still lists every note that shares a word with the question. Those notes are never hidden. What changes is what the answer says about them. **Definition** (`RecallCoverage`, DoddyCore): a question is split into *ideas*, which are its search words with translations and number forms joined ("kod" and "code", "five" and "5"), because the voice agent searches in both languages at once. A note covers an idea when it matched any of its words. If no listed note covers every idea, the answer is **partial**. Any idea no listed note covers is named as the user typed it ("wywóz", not "wywoz"). **Find screen:** the header says "N PARTIAL MATCHES" and a box says "No saved note mentions “netflix”. The notes below match only “password”. Check them before relying on one." When every word is in some note but never in the same note, it says "No single note has all of that." **Voice:** `search_memories` replies "Found N saved notes, but none contains every key word", names the missing words, and still gives every note's exact text. It tells the agent: ignore a missing word that is only a translation of a found one, otherwise tell the user plainly that no note mentions it, and do not present the notes as the answer. The reply text carries this instruction, so `voice-agent/tools.json` and the live agent are unchanged. **Question filler words are no longer searched** ("say", "anything", "leave", "put", "saved", "remember", "find"; Polish "ma", "przez", "coś", "który", "powiedział", "zostawiłem", "położyłem", "zapisałem" and similar). A note never contains them, so every answer to "What did the electrician say?" looked partial. **Benchmark** (ADR-249): a question with no answer now passes when the answer is honest, meaning "no match" or a flagged partial match. An answerable question flagged partial is counted as a false alarm, and the gate fails if a question that wasn't flagged becomes flagged. **Not changed:** "Ask Doddy" (the gateway assistant) still gets partial matches as grounding without the flag; its citation check stays as before | Applied 2026-10-10. Benchmark on Linux (Swift 6.1.2): honest answer to unanswerable questions **5 → 10 of 10** (5 "no match", 5 flagged partial); found 65/66 and first 61/66 unchanged; false alarms **12 of 66** answerable questions. Each of the 12 is literally true but over-cautious: the note uses another word ("sernik" for "cheesecake", "peanuts" for "nut") or another word ending outside the rules ("Ewy", "bramy"). 424 package tests pass (6 new). The Find screen and voice controller only syntax-parse here; they are not compiled (no Xcode) or tried on a device |

## Every version of a corrected note (2026-10-10, owner: "move to 2", research backlog OP-003)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 252 | **Doddy keeps every wording a note has had** (`docs/implementation/MEMORY_HISTORY_STATUS.md`). Before, a correction overwrote the current text, so only the original and the latest wording survived. **Store:** new table `memory_corrections` (migration `v9_memory_corrections` (named `v5_…` before the merge with the lists and photos line, whose `v5`–`v8` come first)). Each correction is one row: note id, number from 1, text and time. Primary key is (note, number). CHECKs refuse a number below 1 and blank text. The row is deleted with its note by a foreign-key cascade, with `secure_delete` on as before. The original stays in `memories.raw_text`. A correction and the note's new text are written in one transaction and both are read back before success. **Upgrade:** each note corrected before this gets one correction, its current wording at its edit time. Wordings in between were never stored and are not invented. A note that is corrected but has no trustworthy history (restored from an older export) first records its current wording, then the new one, so nothing is lost. Corrections that don't end at the note's current wording are never shown as its history. **Search** still uses only the current wording: a corrected-away wording is history, not an answer. **Note screen:** "Earlier versions (N)" lists each in-between wording, newest first, with its time, above "Original capture" (unchanged). A note without full history says so. The delete message says every version is removed. **Exports:** JSON gets a `corrections` list inside format 1. Older Doddy builds ignore it and restore the notes without history. Text and Markdown add an "Earlier" line or block for each in-between wording. A note with a single correction exports exactly as before. **Restore and Undo:** they bring a note back with its corrections when those are its whole history; otherwise the note comes back without them. A damaged correction refuses the whole file, like a damaged note. **Privacy:** corrections are read only by the note screen and the exports, including the encrypted backup (ADR-222, joined at the merge), checked by search. They are never sent to the voice agent, Ask, the server copy or search. "What stays on this iPhone" now lists them | Applied 2026-10-10. Linux (Swift 6.1.2): 437 package tests pass (13 new). They include the upgrade from a real v4 file, cascade delete, undo, an export → restore round trip, refused bad rows, and no wording lost for a note without history; that last test caught a planted bug. `MemoryStore` passes the Linux compile check. The note screen and Settings only syntax-parse here: not compiled (no Xcode) or tried on a device (Mac checklist M12, E6) |

## Unclear days and times (2026-10-10, owner: "move to 3", research backlog OP-014)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 253 | **Doddy asks one question when the user's words for a day or time allow two answers, or don't match the date sent** (`docs/implementation/SAID_TIME_STATUS.md`). Until now the app only saw the finished date ("2026-10-16T09:00"). Turning "next Friday" into a date was left to the model, and the app couldn't know it had guessed. **New optional tool field `said_time`** on `create_reminder`, `create_calendar_event` and `remind_me_about_note`, and inside `propose_plan` steps: the user's own words for when, unchanged. **`SaidTime`** (DoddyCore) checks those words against the date with fixed English and Polish word rules, no model. **It asks when the words allow two answers:** "next Friday" / "w przyszły piątek" (the coming one or the one after; both dates are named), "this weekend" (Saturday or Sunday), "next week" with no day, and "at 7" / "o 7" with nothing saying morning or evening ("07:00 or 19:00"). On the day itself, an hour already gone is not counted, so "today at 7" said at 13:00 means 19:00 with no question. **It asks when a time was added that the user never said:** "tomorrow morning" sent as 09:00. A reminder asks "what time, or is the day without a time fine"; an event or note reminder asks "what time". **It asks when the date contradicts the words:** the wrong weekday, "tomorrow" or "today" or "pojutrze" on another day, a written date ("16 października") on another date, "at 9" sent as 10:00, or "in 2 hours" more than 5 minutes off. **It does not judge words it doesn't read** ("half past seven", "wpół do ósmej", "kwadrans"). Hours and minutes ("9:30") are read as the 24-hour clock. Polish weekday forms are listed in full, because a prefix would read "piątej" (five o'clock) as Friday. The question goes back through the existing `failed: not added yet … Ask the user …` reply, so no card appears and nothing is added before the answer. One unclear step stops the whole plan, as before. **Without `said_time` nothing changes,** so an agent that doesn't send it works as before. On-screen edits to a plan step are not checked, because they are the user's own. Spec: `voice-agent/tools.json` (three new parameters and the plan example) and two prompt lines in `instructions.md` | Applied 2026-10-10. Linux (Swift 6.1.2): 445 package tests pass (8 new phrase-table tests, through the tool parser and the plan card); a planted bug was caught; the voice agent spec check passes. **Synced to the live agent 2026-10-10** (owner: "yes sync the live agent"). `said_time` was added to the live `create_reminder` (`tool_9901m4f2xyn1exfrx6as0kek55rk`), `create_calendar_event` (`tool_8401m4f2xxyre6ps328h08jvj11y`) and `remind_me_about_note` (`tool_7401m4k84jjgf1ctk26vj03kextq`), and to the `propose_plan` example (`tool_3001m4knv27zfw09afmn7sjamfcp`). The two prompt sentences were added in place to the live prompt. The live prompt and tools are ahead of this branch's spec, from other sessions: lists, translator, morning check-in, `repeat` on note reminders, `add_to_list` in plans. All of that was kept unchanged; only the ADR-253 lines were added. Version `agtvrsn_1601m4m03vmqe90rh35280g5pxp7` → `agtvrsn_1601m4m2szrwfb9rhx1r2vcgmc3j` (rollback: the first). The questions have not been heard in a real conversation or tried on a device, and only app builds with ADR-253 act on `said_time`; older builds ignore it |

## Translator island (2026-10-10, owner: "go ahead on a local merge of both branches")

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 244 | **Language Bridge while translating** (board H of the owner's V4 pack, `design/dynamic-island-v4/02-states/language-bridge-0-*.svg`). While translator mode is on (ADR-215), the voice island's moment is `.translating(from:to:bothWays:)`, set by `VoiceSessionController.islandMoment` from the mode's setup: face to face is your language ⇄ theirs, listen only is theirs → yours, "?" until Doddy hears their language. It carries language codes only, never anything said. The island resyncs when the mode or its languages change, not on each line heard. `momentScene` maps it to Language Bridge's first board only: label "TRANSLATE", "Translating", "EN ⇄ FR" (or "EN → FR" for listen only), the codes in the two speech bubbles, "⇄ FR" / "→ FR" compact and "FR" minimal, orange only while Doddy can hear you. The board's second state, "Translation ready / Hear it", is not used: a live conversation has no ready-to-play step. **Bubble words are drawn natively** (`IslandArtLabel`, drawn by `IslandArtView` at the bubbles' centres) rather than kept in the generated art, because the pair changes and the generator would bake in the board's "EN"/"FR". **Private (ADR-215):** `IslandLanguageCode.shown` draws anything that is not a 2–3 letter code as "?", so a phrase passed by mistake never reaches the island; `tools/island_boards/IslandSceneChecks.swift` checks this, case-insensitively, against every word the scene can draw, plus never the second board, muted is not live, stale hides it, and round-trip/older decoding. The Lock Screen card is unchanged ("Talking to Doddy"). Settings' island page now says the island switches design while Doddy translates. | Accepted; Linux checks pass; device check 21 in `docs/implementation/V3_ISLAND_STATUS.md` pending |

## Google Analytics on the website (2026-10-10, owner: "add google analytics to doddy website")

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 245 | **Google Analytics 4 on the landing page and blog, only with consent** (`site/analytics.js`, site README → Analytics). GA sets cookies and the site's audience includes the EU, so nothing from Google loads until the visitor taps **Allow** in a small banner; **No thanks** sends nothing. The choice is kept in `localStorage` on that device; the footer's "Analytics choices" reopens the banner, and switching to No thanks updates GA consent to denied and deletes the `_ga` cookies. Ads storage, ad personalisation and Google signals are off. The measurement ID is one constant (`GA_MEASUREMENT_ID`); while it is empty the file does nothing (no banner, no footer sentence, no request). `/admin/` has no analytics. Vercel Web Analytics (cookieless) stays as it is. CSP in `vercel.json` adds `www.googletagmanager.com` to `script-src` and the GA collection hosts to `img-src`/`connect-src`; the loader is an external same-origin file, so no new inline-script hash | Applied 2026-10-10. Checked in headless Chromium 1194 (Playwright) under the production CSP with a test ID and Google's script stubbed: banner shown on first visit; Allow loads `gtag/js` once and stores the choice; No thanks loads nothing; returning visitors are not asked again; reopening and switching to No thanks clears `_ga` cookies; blog pages show the banner; `/admin/` does not; no horizontal scroll at 390 px, no console or CSP errors. With the ID empty: no banner, no request. The owner's measurement ID `G-WQXYVSGD4Q` is set (2026-10-10). **Amended 15 October:** the ID is now `G-MRF40685L1`, the web stream "doddy website" on a separate GA4 property "doddy" (`properties/559137115`), so Doddy's data no longer sits in the property that holds the Name Atlas stream. The ID is still the one constant in `analytics.js`. **Superseded for the consent part, 15 October, by the owner's decision ("raw tag go"):** `analytics.js` now loads Google's plain tag on every page with no banner, and the footer says Google Analytics measures visits and sets cookies. The consent-first design above no longer applies. The privacy draft's [CONFIRM] items stay open, including the lawful basis for EU visitors. The new stream's default URL is `https://doddy.lol`, while the site's canonical host is `www.doddy.lol`. **Waiting on the owner:** a privacy policy that names Google Analytics. Not checked: real GA collection and the GA dashboard |

## End-of-conversation save suggestions (2026-10-10)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 247 | **"Worth keeping?" after a voice conversation** (owner: "build the end-of-conversation save suggestions next"; `docs/implementation/SAVE_SUGGESTIONS_STATUS.md`). When a conversation ends (End or disconnect, not a failure), the voice screen offers up to 3 sentences **the user said** that look worth keeping. Chosen by fixed rules on the iPhone (`SaveSuggestions`), **not by a model**, so no transcript goes anywhere new and no words are invented: only user transcript lines (never Doddy's lines or tool results); leading fillers ("oh, by the way"), a "remember (that)" prefix and trailing "btw" are trimmed and the first letter capitalised, nothing else changes; questions, requests to Doddy, small talk, sentences under 4 or over 40 words, and secrets (passwords, card/bank PINs and numbers, PESEL, IBAN, any 11+ digit run) are never offered; a sentence needs signals worth 3 points (asked to remember 5; date, number, weekday or month 2; birthday/anniversary 3; recommendation 3; "my …" or a name's 's 2, else a family word 1; plan phrase 2; preference 2; a capitalised name 1); anything covered by a note Doddy put on a save card or plan in this conversation (saved or declined) or by a saved note (all its numbers and 80% of its other words) is not offered. **Every suggestion starts unticked**; the user may change the words (which ticks it); Save runs the normal checked save (`source: explicitVoice`, read back) one by one and shows "saved" only for read-back records, a reason for failures. Offered once per conversation; never for a Private conversation; Settings → What Doddy knows → "Suggest notes after a conversation" (on unless turned off; owner may choose off). No live-agent change. A model could later *choose* among the same sentences (never rewrite them) behind the same card; that needs its own approval | Applied 2026-10-10. Rules and card state tested on Linux (synthetic transcripts); the controller's new methods type-checked against the real `MemoryStore`; the voice screen and controller file **not compiled** (ElevenLabs/AVFoundation) and **not tried** with real transcripts: Mac checklist V8–V10 |

## Siri reads a note back (2026-10-10)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 248 | **"Ask Doddy what I saved"** (owner: "build the Siri note readback next"; `docs/implementation/SIRI_READBACK_STATUS.md`). New App Shortcut `AskDoddyNoteIntent` (phrases "Ask Doddy what I saved", "What did I save in Doddy", "Read my note from Doddy", "Check my notes in Doddy"); Siri asks "What are you looking for?", the app (not opened, `openAppWhenRun = false`, main app target) runs the **same on-device recall as Memories → Find** (`AppEnvironment.recallService`, now one shared `AppEnvironment.shared` per process) and Siri speaks a reply built by fixed rules (`SiriReadback`): the note's own words in quotes and when it was saved ("today", "yesterday", "on 3 October"); with several matches the two closest and how many more; notes that give different numbers are both read with "Check which one is current", never one picked; when no note covers the whole question, Siri first says which words no note has and reads the closest note as only a partial match (ADR-251); no match is "I couldn't find that in your saved memories."; long notes cut at a word with "open Doddy for the rest". **Requires an unlocked iPhone** (`authenticationPolicy = .requiresAuthentication`), because notes hold codes and addresses. Settings → Back Tap and Siri → "Siri can read my notes aloud", on unless turned off; off answers that it is off and reads nothing. Privacy: Doddy searches on the iPhone; Siri is Apple's and handles the request and the spoken answer under the user's Siri settings — new "Apple (Siri)" row in What leaves this iPhone and a sentence in the Settings privacy paragraph. Siri can't change or save anything here. | Applied 2026-10-10. Reply rules tested on Linux (`SiriReadbackTests`); `SiriNoteLookup` type-checks against DoddyCore; the intent, shortcut and Settings switch **not compiled** and **not tried** with Siri: Mac checklist S7–S11 |

## "Hey Doddy" wake word (2026-10-11)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 254 | **"Hey Doddy": an opt-in wake word that starts a conversation, with a greeting on the Dynamic Island** (owner: "create a Dodi that can listen in the background and can be waken up on a call, hey Dodi, and then … the Dodi dynamic island pop-up shows up and says, hey bud, what's up? … for me right now … just Pat"; owner chose "Own listening mode" over Siri-only, and "Picovoice (Recommended)" as the detector; `docs/implementation/HEY_DODDY_STATUS.md`). **Conflict, decided by the owner:** the README's "no covert always-on mic", PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS M3 "no ambient mic" and "no perpetual Dynamic Island", and ADR-207's "no wake word" are relaxed **for this opt-in, overt mode only**: off unless turned on, the island and iOS's microphone dot show while it listens, and Stop listening is on the island. **What iOS allows (and so what is promised):** third-party apps can't have a system wake word; only Siri can. Doddy can listen while it is open and keep listening in the background (existing `audio` background mode) until iOS ends the app, Low Power Mode, a call or another app's recording, or the user stops it; after a restart or after iOS closed it, Doddy must be opened again. **Detector:** Picovoice Porcupine (on-device; package pinned by revision, `WakeWordListener.swift` is the only file that may use it, guard script rule). The user brings their **own Picovoice AccessKey** (Keychain, this device only) and **"Hey Doddy" keyword file** (.ppn from the Picovoice Console, English, iOS; copied to Application Support, not backed up); nothing is in the repository. Picovoice checks the key online; no audio leaves the iPhone until the wake word. **After the wake word:** the listener lets go of the microphone, the island (one Live Activity shared with the conversation, because iOS can't start one in the background) shows "Hey Pat, what's up?" (nickname, else first word of the About you name, else "bud"; Polish "Hej Pat, co słychać?"; cleaned like a nickname), and a normal `VoiceSessionController` conversation starts (same notes, profile, privacy and approvals as a tap). The greeting stays on the island until the user first speaks. The voice screen opens if Doddy is on screen, or when the app is next opened while it runs or left suggestions. When it ends, listening starts again on the same island; if iOS refuses in the background, the island ends and Settings says to open Doddy. **Safeguards against false wake-ups (they cost ElevenLabs minutes):** detections are ignored for 4 s after listening (re)starts; a wake-started conversation with no word from the user ends after 20 s; more than 12 wake-ups in an hour rests the listener until the hour has passed. Paused for a conversation, the voice screen, Doddy's own sound (brief read aloud, voice samples), Low Power Mode and interruptions. **Spoken greeting:** the app now sends dynamic variable `opening_line` with every conversation (the greeting after the wake word, otherwise the usual opening); `voice-agent/session.json` first message is `{{opening_line}}` with that placeholder. **Applied to the live agent 2026-10-11** (owner: "you do it"): English and Polish first message `{{opening_line}}`, placeholder = the previous live text "Hey, it's Doddy. what's up?"; other presets and placeholders unchanged; version `agtvrsn_6701m4mc9b7wekgb2jeg1c6pwnyv`, rollback `agtvrsn_5701m4m4268zfx2s83k8xcdg5bgm`. Microphone usage text, Settings privacy paragraph, Microphone row and What leaves this iPhone (Picovoice row) updated. **Licence:** Porcupine's free plan is for personal use; any commercial release needs a Picovoice plan (owner decision). App Store review risk (guidelines on background audio and always-on listening) not assessed with Apple | Applied 2026-10-11 on Linux only. DoddyCore `WakeWordTests` (20) and a DataFlow test pass; `scripts/ci.sh --core-only` PASS (511 DoddyCore tests). The island state (old content decodes, greeting precedence, wake-listening flags) checked in a Swift 6 harness with an ActivityKit stand-in. Guard script FAILs when Porcupine is imported elsewhere. **iOS app NOT built**: the listener, Porcupine package resolution, background restart after a conversation, the island hand-off and false-wake rate are unverified — Mac checklist H1–H14 |

Fitted to the default branch: the island uses the user's island style (ADR-239) and the scene model, with "HEY DODDY" as its label and no timer while waiting; listening also steps aside for read aloud (ADR-243) and voice-memo recording and playback (ADR-230), each tracked separately; a conversation the wake word starts opens on screen only when no other sheet is up, the same rule as listening on open (ADR-225). The branch's morning brief read-aloud hook was dropped with the brief. Port checked on Linux only (Swift 6.2.1): `scripts/ci.sh --core-only` PASS, 608 package tests; island scene checks for the waiting state PASS. iOS app NOT built.

Undo is the same checked change backwards (`EventChange.reversed`; for reminders reopen, complete, or the old due, cleared if there was none). The item must still look exactly as Doddy left it, or nothing is written ("changed elsewhere since, so Doddy left it alone"). The result is read back and Doddy is told, as an "(app)" message, what was put back, or that it couldn't be confirmed. The app finds exactly one match, or the agent asks which one (none found, or several). The card shows what goes, says a repeating event loses only this time, and says Doddy can't undo it (Undo, ADR-258, covers changes, not deletes: putting back a deleted event would make a new one, without its guests or history). Nothing is deleted until **Delete**. At the tap: permission and connection switch again, the item must still look exactly as on the card, then `remove(span: .thisEvent)` / `remove(commit:)`, then Calendar or Reminders is read again; the agent hears "deleted" only when the item is gone, otherwise "unknown". Logged in What Doddy did; paused with Calendar or Reminders. One item per card; deleting several at once is still not built. `docs/implementation/DELETE_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; live agent updated |
| 260 | **Alarms by voice (AlarmKit, iOS 26).** A phone tool `set_alarm`: `time` (the first day and time, local, later than now), optional `repeat` (`every_day`, `weekdays`, `weekends`) and `label`. It belongs to the existing **Timers** connection (now "timers and alarms"), so the same switch and Pause all cover it. The card shows the time, the day or days and the label, and says it rings even in Silent mode or a Focus; nothing is set until **Set alarm**. At the tap: the switch, the time again, AlarmKit permission (asked the first time, with `NSAlarmKitUsageDescription`), then `AlarmManager.schedule`, then AlarmKit's list is read: "added" only when it lists the alarm, else "unknown". On iOS older than 26, or built with an SDK without AlarmKit, the agent is told before any card and offers a timer or a reminder; if alarms were refused, it says where to allow them. **Settings › Alarms** lists Doddy's alarms that AlarmKit still has, with **Turn off** (checked gone). AlarmKit only in `DoddyApp/Features/Alarms` (guard). Doddy keeps only each alarm's time text and label in this iPhone's settings, to name it in the list. The permission prompts for Calendar and Reminders now also name Delete (ADR-259). `docs/implementation/ALARMS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode 26; never run on a device; live agent updated |
| 261 | **Evening wrap-up.** Off by default (Settings › Evening wrap-up, 21:00 to start with). A daily notification says only "Good evening · Anything to move to tomorrow?" and opens a screen listing the open reminders in the Reminders app due today or earlier (oldest first, at most 30; undated ones aren't offered), read on this iPhone only with Reminders on in Connections and not paused. **None is ticked to start with**; **Move N to tomorrow** moves only the ticked ones. Each keeps its time (a day stays a day), lands on the day after now, and is written only if it still looks as shown, then read back — the same checked change as ADR-229. A time the clocks skip or repeat tomorrow isn't guessed; it stays and is counted as not moved. The receipt says how many moved ("Checked in Reminders") and how many didn't. Nothing is sent anywhere; no streaks or nagging. `docs/implementation/EVENING_WRAPUP_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode |
| 262 | **Watch complications and the Smart Stack.** A watchOS widget extension, `DoddyWatchWidgets`, embedded in the watch app: circular, corner, inline and rectangular complications, also offered in the Smart Stack. The watch app saves each snapshot the iPhone sends (ADR-232) to the watch's App Group folder (`WidgetSnapshotFile`, read back) and reloads the timelines; "Show in widgets" off arrives as an empty snapshot, so nothing of the user's stays there. **A watch face is seen by anyone nearby and stays on with Always On, so complications show only the next Doddy reminder's time, the number of open list items and the name of the list with the most left — never a note's or reminder's words, and never list items.** Smart Stack relevance is high in the hour before the next reminder, low when lists have things left, none otherwise; timeline entries an hour before and just after each reminder. App Group opt-in like the iPhone's (`DODDY_WATCH_ENTITLEMENTS`); without it the complication says to open Doddy on the iPhone. `docs/implementation/WATCH_COMPLICATIONS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never run on a watch |
| 263 | **Shared lists through iCloud** (owner: "iCloud sharing"). Off unless the build is set up for it (paid team, iCloud container, `DODDY_CLOUD_CONTAINER` and an `-iCloud` entitlements file); otherwise nothing is offered and CloudKit is never touched. A list's menu has **Share with people…**: after a confirmation that the name and items go to the user's iCloud and to whoever they invite, the list is copied to its own zone in their private CloudKit database (one record for the name, one per item), read back, and the zone is shared (`CKShare(recordZoneID:)`, no public access); Apple's sharing screen invites people. Someone who opens the link sees **Join a shared list?** with the list name and who shared it; nothing is joined until **Join list**. Sync, when Doddy comes to the front, when a shared list changes here, and from **Update now**: read iCloud, three-way merge with this iPhone and the last sync (`SharedListMerge`: a one-sided change wins; the same field changed on both sides keeps iCloud's version; an edit beats a delete; all adds kept), write each side only if it changed (iCloud: only records still as fetched, all or nothing), read both back, and only then remember the merge as the last sync — a failed or unconfirmed sync remembers nothing and is retried. What comes from iCloud is checked like typed text. **Stop sharing** (owner) deletes the iCloud copy and everyone loses it; **Leave** (participant) takes this user off it; either way the list stays on this iPhone, unshared, and deleting a shared list stops sharing first. If a list stops being shared elsewhere, the next sync says so and keeps it here. No push: others' changes arrive when Doddy is open. New: migration `v10_list_shares`, `ListRepository.replaceShared`, CloudKit only in `DoddyApp/Features/Sharing` (guard), What leaves this iPhone has an iCloud row, the Privacy paragraph a sentence. Its migration is `v10_list_shares`, after the default branch's `v9_memory_corrections` (ADR-252). `docs/implementation/SHARED_LISTS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never run against CloudKit |

## OpenAI Realtime test voice (2026-10-11, owner: "a button on main screen that would switch doddy between realtime model from open ai and eleven labs")

Numbering: written as ADR-235 on `claude/blissful-einstein-5msya4`; renumbered to ADR-265 when merged with the default branch (ADR-264 had been taken by Whole Doddy).

| # | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 265 | **Test switch on Home: ElevenLabs or OpenAI Realtime runs Doddy's voice** (owner: "a button on main screen that would switch doddy between realtime model from open ai and eleven labs … i want to feel a difference"). A segmented switch under Talk to Doddy, read when the next conversation starts; ElevenLabs stays the default. **No second database:** notes, lists, conversations, profile and every tool live on this iPhone, and both providers get the same rendered prompt (`voice-agent/instructions.md` with the same variables), the same 32 tools and the same confirmation cards, so the only difference is the provider. That is what makes the comparison fair, and what you save with one is known to the other. How: (1) `server/voice-token` takes an optional body `{"provider":"openai"}` and mints a 120 s OpenAI Realtime client secret with only model and voice (`OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL` default `gpt-realtime`, `OPENAI_REALTIME_VOICE` default `marin`); no body = the ElevenLabs request exactly as before. The prompt, notes and tools go from the phone straight to OpenAI, never through the server. (2) DoddyCore `OpenAIRealtimeWire`: session.update (semantic VAD, eagerness high, PCM 24 kHz, `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` for the user's lines), tool output, app messages, greeting from `session.json`, truncate on barge-in, event parsing (GA Realtime event names). (3) App: `OpenAIRealtimeSession` (WebSocket with the client secret, `AVAudioEngine` with Apple voice processing for echo cancellation) behind a small `LiveVoiceLink` protocol that the ElevenLabs `Conversation` also conforms to, so the ElevenLabs path keeps its exact calls. OpenAI function calls become the same `ClientToolCallEvent` and run through the same `handle`. (4) Not the same: weather, Wikipedia and place lookup are ElevenLabs server tools, answered "not available in the OpenAI test" so Doddy says so instead of inventing; ElevenLabs voice choice, speed and expressiveness don't apply (voice is the server's `OPENAI_REALTIME_VOICE`); no hang-up tool; mid-conversation language switching is the model's own. (5) Honesty: the voice screen says "Test voice: OpenAI runs this conversation" while it does; What leaves this iPhone gets an "OpenAI (test voice)" row that is active only when the switch says OpenAI and a token server is set; the microphone prompt names OpenAI (Polish updated); each remembered conversation records which voice ran it. OpenAI's retention of Realtime audio/transcripts is **not verified** and the screen says so. (6) Guard: networking allowed in one more file, `Features/Voice/OpenAIRealtimeSession.swift`. Details and the test protocol: `docs/implementation/VOICE_PROVIDER_TEST_STATUS.md`. | Built for testing. Token server and DoddyCore tested on Linux; **app code not compiled with Xcode, never connected to OpenAI, never heard on a device.** Owner: deploy the token server with `OPENAI_API_KEY`, set a spending limit on the OpenAI project |

## Undo in "What Doddy did" (2026-10-11)

At the tap: Pause all, the connection switch and iOS permission are checked; the item must still look exactly as Doddy left it (an added reminder is found only by Doddy's marker, so the user's same-named reminder is never touched), or nothing is written and the row says it was left alone. After the write, a read-back decides "Undone"; otherwise "not sure", and Undo is not offered again. Retry only when nothing was written. **Conflict:** ADR-229 said Doddy never deletes; Undo removes only an item Doddy added in the last day, unchanged, after confirmation (owner to accept, or limit Undo to changes). **Fixed on the way:** checked changes ("changed:", "completed:") were listed as "Not sure it happened"; now "Done". Not by voice; not for notes, lists, plan steps or online accounts; strings English only | Applied 2026-10-11 on Linux only (Swift 6.1.2): `ActivityUndoTests` (12) pass; a planted bug was caught; `scripts/ci.sh --core-only` PASS (620 DoddyCore tests). **Owner answers, 2026-10-11** (asked in the session, both accepted): **ADR-268** — Undo may remove an event or reminder Doddy itself added in the last day, unchanged, after the user confirms ("Undo may remove items"); this narrows ADR-229's "never deletes" to that case. **ADR-269** — when the user asks about birthdays, each contact's name with the day and month (never year or age) goes to ElevenLabs ("Birthdays to voice service"). Still open: syncing the four new tools to the live agent; an exchange-rate provider for currencies.

Merged 2026-10-11 with the default line (PR #25), which had taken ADR-255 to ADR-263 in the meantime; this branch's ADR-255 to ADR-262 became ADR-264 to ADR-274, ADR-265 to ADR-273 after PR #28 took ADR-264 (Whole Doddy), ADR-267 to ADR-274 after PR #31 and PR #32 took ADR-265 (OpenAI test voice) and ADR-266 (cream Lock Screen cards), and **ADR-268 to ADR-275** after PR #33 took ADR-267 (Whole Doddy as the default island style). List Undo, built after that, is ADR-276 (decisions, status docs, code comments). **Two Undo features, one rule:** the default line's ADR-258 offers Undo for a change on the voice screen for 10 minutes; ADR-268 offers Undo in What Doddy did for a day, also for events and reminders Doddy added, and ADR-271 by voice. Both check the item still looks exactly as Doddy left it, so nothing is undone twice; after either succeeds, the other is cleared (the row is marked undone, or the on-screen receipt is dismissed). Both use one `EventChange.reversed` and one occurrence-id format (`CalendarEventSummary.occurrenceID`). The default line's delete replies ("deleted: …") now count as Done in What Doddy did (they read "Not sure"), and its new rows ("Delete event", "Delete reminder", "Set alarm") have Polish titles. ADR-259's "Doddy can't undo a delete" stands: `undo_last` says it can't undo a delete.

**Live agent, 2026-10-11** (owner: "update the live agent"): the four tools were created on agent `agent_0501m4et27vyeawrwqfdxxfszfa6` (`upcoming_birthdays` `tool_4501m4n80mxyfb3b0fhbeveh5ddb`, `calculate` `tool_7001m4n80pe0f7wrkgp6kcv9h5wj`, `convert_units` `tool_3801m4n80q65ekpb1zc8mhst0zp0`, `undo_last` `tool_6501m4n80qyqee09pksxpdztmy18`) and attached (39 tools), with the prompt lines from `voice-agent/instructions.md`. The live agent had drifted from the default line's spec in four small places, now synced to the repo: the delete line's "Never say it was deleted unless the result starts with "deleted"", `change_calendar_event` / `change_reminder` descriptions ("use delete_… for that"), and `remind_me_about_note`'s `list`/`note` wording. Versions `agtvrsn_8201m4mf5fqhf9087wkrjz8kjenp` (before) → `agtvrsn_1801m4n83rzdfbr8re9f8q599bm8` → `agtvrsn_8601m4n87tvmf83azq8ek4wtpst4` (rollback: the first). Checked with `scripts/voice_agent_spec.py compare` on a fresh export: **MATCH**. Not yet tried in a real conversation; the tools only work with an app build that has this branch.
