## Round 3 (2026-10-11, owner: "do all of them")
Ten items: a first build (blocked in this container), then nine features. Numbered ADR-280 to ADR-289: the default branch reached ADR-265 while this round was being built, and ADR-266 to ADR-279 were left free for other lines: the default branch has since used 266 and 267 (cream Lock Screen cards, Whole Doddy default), and 268 to 279 remain for `claude/affectionate-edison-ktc6i6`, which is waiting to be joined with eight decisions of its own. Commit messages on this branch before the renumbering give 264 to 273 (+16).

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 280 | **Several changes on one card.** Two phone tools: `delete_calendar_events` (`from`/`to`, local; a day alone as `to` is the whole day; at most 31 days; events that *start* in the range) and `move_reminders` (`due_by` day → `to` day; open reminders due on or before it, each keeping its time; a time the clocks skip that day is left out and named). One card lists every item, all ticked because the user asked for all; any can be unticked, and the button counts the ticked ones. At most 20 items, otherwise the agent asks for a shorter range. Each ticked item runs on its own through the single-item path (ADR-229, ADR-259): unchanged check, change, read-back. The answer starts with the verb only when every ticked item changed ("deleted", "moved"); otherwise "partly …" naming the rest, or "unknown"/"failed". Deletes can't be undone from Doddy, and the card says so. `docs/implementation/BULK_CHANGES_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; live agent pending |
| 281 | **An event's place and notes by voice.** `change_calendar_event` also takes `new_location` and `add_note`, alone or with a new time or title. The place now is read on this iPhone only to show it on the card ("Place: old → new") and to check it hasn't changed since; it is never sent to the agent, whose reply names only the new place. A note is only ever added as a new last line, never replaces or deletes the notes ("Its notes are only added to, never replaced."). Undo (ADR-258) puts the place back and takes the added line off again, only if the notes still end with it. Limits: 200 characters for a place, 500 for a note. `docs/implementation/EVENT_DETAILS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; live agent pending |
| 282 | **Repeating events and reminders by voice.** `create_calendar_event` and `create_reminder` take `repeat` (`daily`, `weekdays`, `weekly`, `every_two_weeks`, `monthly`, `yearly`) and `repeat_until` (a day, not before the first date). It is Apple's own repeat (`EKRecurrenceRule`), not Doddy's note reminders. The card shows "Repeats every week until …"; nothing is added until Add. "added" now also needs the repeat to read back the same: a rule Doddy doesn't write, or none, doesn't confirm. A reminder repeat needs a due. Editing a plan step keeps its repeat. `docs/implementation/REPEATS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode; live agent pending |
| 283 | **Alarms by voice, part 2.** `list_alarms` reads Doddy's own alarms (time and label) on the phone, no card. `cancel_alarm` (`time` HH:MM, `label` only if two share the time) finds exactly one, shows "Turn off this alarm?" (a repeating one stops for every day), and turns it off only on Turn off, checked by AlarmKit no longer listing it. Every Doddy alarm now has **Snooze**: it rings again after 9 minutes, with a countdown on the Lock Screen and in the Dynamic Island drawn by a new `AlarmLiveActivity` in the widget extension. Doddy can't see or change the Clock app's alarms, and says so. The shared `DoddyAlarmMetadata` moves to `Shared/Alarms`; the guard allows AlarmKit there and in the snooze widget only. `docs/implementation/ALARMS_STATUS.md` (part 2). | Built; not compiled with Xcode 26; live agent pending |
| 284 | **Shared lists update straight away.** While any list is shared, Doddy registers for silent notifications (no permission prompt; the device token goes only to Apple, not to any Doddy server — `PushRegistrar` drops tokens nobody asked for) and saves one `CKDatabaseSubscription` per database that has a shared list (private for lists the user shares, shared for lists they joined), read back. When someone changes a shared list, iCloud wakes Doddy with a silent notification that carries no list content, and Doddy runs the usual checked sync (ADR-263). Needs the push entitlement (`Config/Doddy-Push-AppGroup-iCloud.entitlements`) and adds the `remote-notification` background mode, used for nothing else. Without push, nothing changes: updates arrive when Doddy is opened, and the list says which. The subscriptions are removed when no list of that kind is shared any more. `docs/implementation/SHARED_LISTS_STATUS.md` (part 2). | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never run against iCloud |
| 285 | **"Added by Marta" on shared lists.** When Doddy adds an item to a shared list it writes `addedBy` (this user's iCloud user record name) on the item's record; when it ticks one, `doneBy`; unticking clears it. Other devices fall back to the record's creator for items added before this. Names come from the share's participants (`PersonNameComponentsFormatter`, short). A row shows "Added by Marta · Ticked by Tom" only for other people; your own changes get no label, and someone with no known name is "someone else", never guessed. Kept in memory on this iPhone, refreshed at each sync; nothing is stored in the database, and nothing of it goes to the voice agent. `docs/implementation/SHARED_LISTS_STATUS.md` (part 3). | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never run against iCloud |
| 286 | **A list from a photo.** New list has **Read a list from a photo**: Apple's photo picker (no photo library permission), the text read on this iPhone with Vision (photo notes' reader, ADR-218, language correction off), and `PhotoList.proposal` turns it into items: bullets, numbers and checkboxes at a line's start dropped, repeats dropped, words as read; a first line ending in ":" becomes the suggested name (only if the name is empty). The items go into the New list editor with "Read N lines from the photo. Check them before Create." — nothing is saved until Create, and the photo isn't kept. Lines over 200 characters or past 200 items are left out and counted. The picker stays in `Features/Photos` (guard). `docs/implementation/PHOTO_LIST_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode |
| 287 | **Talking to Doddy on the Apple Watch: adding to a list.** A list on the watch now has **Add an item** at the top: said with the watch's own dictation or written, checked on the watch with the same rules as a typed item, then sent to the iPhone (`WatchRequest.addToList`). The iPhone adds it through the same store as on the phone and replies from the read-back: "Added “oat milk” on your iPhone.", "already on the list", or why not. Nothing is queued: an unreachable iPhone means nothing happens and the watch says so (ADR-232). With saving a dictated note and ticking items, which the watch already did, the watch covers the three things people do there by voice. A full voice conversation on the watch is not built: the ElevenLabs voice runs on the iPhone. `docs/implementation/APPLE_WATCH_STATUS.md` (round 3). | Built; not compiled with Xcode; never run on a watch |
| 288 | **Birthday reminders from contacts.** Settings › **Birthdays** lists contacts that have a birthday (next first), each with its own switch, **all off**. A picked one becomes Doddy's own yearly notification at 09:00 on the day (29 February on 28 February), read back from iOS; at most 20 people (iOS allows 64 pending notifications for the whole app). Only name and birthday are read, on this iPhone, with Contacts on in Connections; nothing goes to the voice agent, a server, Calendar or Reminders. The notification shows the name only when notifications may show note text; otherwise "A birthday today". The request id never contains the contact id. Re-applied at launch so names and dates follow Contacts. The Contacts permission prompt now names birthdays. `docs/implementation/BIRTHDAYS_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode |
| 289 | **Offline.** When a conversation can't start or stops because the iPhone has no connection (the token server's `offline`, or a URL error inside the SDK's error: no internet, lost connection, host not found, timed out, roaming off), the voice screen says **You're offline** in plain words instead of a technical error, and offers what works without the internet: a note written or dictated there, saved on this iPhone only after **Save note** and read back by the store ("Saved on this iPhone: …"). Nothing is queued to send later, and the agent is not involved. Doddy doesn't watch the network itself: the capability guard forbids the Network framework, so it recognises the errors it already gets. `docs/implementation/OFFLINE_STATUS.md`. | Built; not compiled with Xcode |

## The greeting waves (2026-10-11)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 290 | **Whole Doddy greets with a wave** (owner: "use the wave pose for the greeting"; changes ADR-264's "arms out"). The pack's only wave is a strip on paper (`016_doddy_wave_v01.png`), so `tools/whole_doddy/cut_wave.py` cuts out frame 4 by removing only the paper connected to the frame's edges: nothing is redrawn or recoloured, and the cream face and shoes stay. New `IslandPose.wave` and `WholeDoddy_wave` image sets; the greeting (connecting or listening with a greeting) shows it until the user first speaks. The arms-out pose and its image sets are removed. Provenance in `docs/design/UPLOADED_MEMORY_ASSETS.md`. | Linux: island checks PASS (greeting waves while connecting and listening). **iOS build NOT RUN**; Mac checklist W5. |
| 291 | **"Open camera" opens the iPhone's Camera app through a Shortcut** (owner, 2026-10-11: asked whether Doddy can open the camera on "open camera", then chose this option over camera capture inside Doddy). iOS gives other apps no public way to open the Camera app, so Doddy uses the existing `run_shortcut` tool with the fixed name **"Doddy Camera"**: a Shortcut the user makes once with the action Open App → Camera. Prompt-only change: one tool line and one word in the strict rules of `voice-agent/instructions.md`, and one sentence in `run_shortcut`'s `name` description in `tools.json`. No app code changed. The existing card still asks the user to tap Run, and Shortcuts opens for a moment before Camera. Doddy never reaches the camera or the photos: no `NSCameraUsageDescription`, and the capability guard still refuses camera APIs, so ADR-224 stands. The reply is the existing "opened: asked Shortcuts to run …": Doddy can't see whether the Camera app opened and must not say a photo was taken. Without the Shortcut, Shortcuts shows its own error, and the prompt tells Doddy to explain the setup once when the user says the camera didn't open. | Applied to the spec 2026-10-11; `voice_agent_spec.py check` passes. Live agent, 2026-10-11 (owner: "Zgoda"): the `run_shortcut` tool (`tool_1601m4gj37f5etbres37pgtvrq3c`) has the new `name` sentence, updated in place and read back. The prompt then went live on the owner's "zrob to ty" (the first try had been refused by this session's safety check): both edits made in place in the live prompt, which is ahead of `instructions.md`; nothing else changed and the tool list is the same 49. Version `agtvrsn_8101m4nf7332e9d9s4vcvvyd1zbx` (rollback) → `agtvrsn_8701m4nqkm9sezbaqxeyddwrey89`, read back after saving. The OpenAI test voice picks the change up with the next app build. Not tried on a device |
| 299 | **More of Doddy's world in the app** (owner: "Do all", the eight additions proposed after ADR-290). All art from the owner's pack, cut out, cropped or downscaled only (`tools/whole_doddy/`; provenance and hashes in `docs/design/UPLOADED_MEMORY_ASSETS.md`). (1) The Lock Screen card shows the island's Whole Doddy pose for the same moment, whatever island style is picked (fist pump at the end only if a note was saved). (2) Read aloud: new `IslandPose.bellyReading`; with Whole Doddy the read-aloud island shows it (paused: cross-legged, finished: fist pump, lost: facepalm). `DoddyToolAttributes.ContentState` gains an optional `islandStyle`, stamped when sent; older content decodes. (3) Find's "no match" is the shrug; a failed save and notes that can't load show the facepalm beside the message (`ProblemView(showsDoddy:)`); the hello pictures are the wave and "on the way" the tiptoe (`CandidateCharacterView`). (4) Scenery behind the top of Home (room), Memories (memory library) and the morning check-in (coffee shop): day pictures in light appearance, night in dark, faint and fading (`SceneryBackdrop`). (5) The voice screen shows the island's pose for the moment (`VoiceScreenArt`); while Doddy speaks the six talking frames of strip 020 play in turn. They follow the "speaking" state only, not the audio. Reduce Motion: the first frame, still. (6) Widgets: Talk to Doddy shows the wave, Next reminder (small) the reach; full-colour Home Screens only, tinted and clear ones keep the symbol. (7) Watch complications: Doddy's face in the circular fallback and beside "Doddy" in the rectangular one, on full-colour faces and tinted ones from watchOS 11. (8) The welcome screen's picture is the 038 page-turn storyboard as four cross-faded panels, once, ending on Doddy waving; Reduce Motion shows the last panel only. The opening (ADR-225) is unchanged. Every picture is decorative, hidden from VoiceOver, and hidden with "Hide Doddy's pictures" in the app. | Linux: island checks PASS, including new read-aloud, voice-screen and image-set checks (planted bugs caught); `swiftc -parse` on every changed file; `scripts/ci.sh --core-only` PASS. **iOS, widget and watch builds NOT RUN** (no Xcode); Mac checklist 4h (X1–X8). Not tried on a device |
| 308 | **Scenery never sits under text** (owner, on the ADR-299 mockups: "lower the scenery so text is clean"; changes ADR-299 item 4). `SceneryBackdrop` is fainter (40 % → 25 %) and fades in at its top as well as out at its bottom. It moves to where there is no text: on Home, behind Doddy's picture only (270 pt, edge to edge), no longer behind the greeting; on Memories and the morning check-in, a 110 pt edge-to-edge strip between the heading and the content, no longer behind the heading. `focus` picks which part of the picture shows (the room's lamp and shelves, the library's shelves, the coffee shop's awning). With "Hide Doddy's pictures" the strips take no space. Pictures unchanged. | Linux: `swiftc -parse` on the changed files; core CI PASS. Mockups board updated. **iOS build NOT RUN**; Mac checklist X4 updated. Not tried on a device |
| 309 | **The camera shortcut is easy to add** (owner, 2026-10-11: "how do we make it easy for the user", then chose a shared shortcut over camera capture in Doddy). Settings → Connections → Shortcuts gains **Open the camera**. When `CameraShortcut.installLink` holds the owner's shared iCloud copy of "Doddy Camera", the section shows **Add camera shortcut**, which opens that link: Shortcuts shows the steps and the user taps Add Shortcut. Until the owner shares one, the link is nil, and the section shows the three steps and an **Open Shortcuts** button instead. The link is checked to be an iCloud Shortcuts share link (`https://www.icloud.com/shortcuts/` + 32 hex), so the button can't open anything else. A test fails if the name in DoddyCore and the one in `instructions.md` and `tools.json` drift apart. "Open the camera" is added to the Shortcuts examples. **The same help is on the voice card** (owner: "how do we show users this setting easily", then "do 1 and 2"). When Doddy proposes running "Doddy Camera" (`CameraShortcut.isCamera`, any case or spacing), a small button sits under Run: **"Don't have the shortcut? Add it"** with the link, or **"Show me how"**, which opens the steps and Open Shortcuts. It is always shown because Doddy can't tell whether the shortcut exists; it is small so it doesn't compete with Run. One view, `CameraShortcutHelp`, draws both places. The prompt's "camera didn't open" line now sends the user to that button, or to Settings, Connections, Shortcuts; this change is in `instructions.md` only. The live agent keeps the ADR-291 wording until a build with the section ships, so it doesn't send users to a screen they don't have yet. Doddy still can't tell whether the shortcut exists: a Shortcuts error callback might say so, but that is untested on a device and not built. No camera access; ADR-224 stands. | Applied 2026-10-11 on branch `claude/camera-shortcut-button`. DoddyCore: 749 tests pass on Linux (Swift 6.1.2) after merging the default branch, the three new camera shortcut tests among them; `swiftc -parse` passes for `CameraShortcutHelp.swift`, `ConnectionsView.swift` and `VoiceView.swift`. The SwiftUI section is **not built** (no Xcode). **Owner action:** share the "Doddy Camera" shortcut as an iCloud link and set `CameraShortcut.installLink` |



## Round 4 (2026-10-11, owner: "do all of them")
Ten items. Two were already done when this round started: item 1, joining `claude/affectionate-edison-ktc6i6`, was merged by another session as PR #27, and that line's follow-ups (its ADR-277 and ADR-278) are still being worked on there. Item 9, the "Talk to Doddy" control for Control Center, the Lock Screen and the Action Button, already existed as `TalkToDoddyControl` (iOS 18). The other eight are ADR-300 to ADR-307. ADR-279 is left free; 290 is the greeting wave; 291 and 299 were taken on the default branch while this round was being built ("open camera", Doddy's world), so this round is ADR-300 to ADR-307. Nothing in this round has been compiled with Xcode.

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 300 | **Currency conversion by voice.** <ul><li>**Tool:** `convert_currency` (`amount`, `from`, `to` as ISO codes; a few unambiguous names such as "zloty", "euro" or "pound" are mapped, while "dollar" alone must be a code).</li><li>**Rates:** the European Central Bank's euro reference rates. The iPhone downloads them from one fixed public file (`ExchangeRateSource.url`). The request carries nothing about the user: no account, no key, no amount. They are fetched at most every 6 hours and the last good copy is kept on the iPhone.</li><li>**Answer:** the converted amount, the rate and the ECB date. Banks and cards use their own rates, and the answer says so. If newer rates couldn't be fetched, it says they are the last ones the app has. With no rates it fails and never guesses.</li><li>**Network:** the only new network file is `ExchangeRateClient.swift`. The capability guard allows URLSession there and refuses any other address in that file.</li><li>Paused connections stop it. It gets a data-flow row and a privacy sentence.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`CurrencyTests`). The ECB file has not been fetched from an iPhone. |
| 301 | **"Where did I park?"** <ul><li>**Tool:** `parking_spot`, with `action` save, find or clear.</li><li>**Save:** reads one position once, only then, with While Using location and at most 100 m uncertainty (place reminders' reader, ADR-196). It shows a card, and the spot is kept on this iPhone only after Save, then read back. One spot at a time; a new one replaces the old.</li><li>**Find:** the agent hears when it was saved, the user's note and a rounded straight-line distance, never coordinates. The screen has Open in Maps, which opens only on a tap.</li><li>**Clear:** removes the spot and checks it is gone.</li><li>Nothing tracks the user.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`ParkingTests`). Location and Maps NOT RUN. |
| 302 | **A reminder's details by voice.** <ul><li>**Parameters:** `change_reminder` also takes `add_note` (a new last line; notes are only added to), `priority` (high, medium, low or none, as Reminders stores them) and `move_to_list`. The list must be exactly one of the user's writable Reminders lists, otherwise nothing is guessed.</li><li>**Combining:** these can go with a new due. "done" stays on its own.</li><li>**Card and write:** the card shows each change. At the tap the reminder must still have the same title, due, open state, priority and list. Everything is written in one save and read back.</li><li>**Undo:** not offered for these changes, and the card says so.</li><li>The log names which details changed, never the note's text.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`ReminderDetailsTests`). EventKit NOT RUN. |
| 303 | **Todoist: complete and move a task.** <ul><li>**Tools:** `complete_todoist_task` (`TODOIST_CLOSE_TASK_V1`) and `move_todoist_task` (`TODOIST_UPDATE_TASK`), both writes with a card. The agent gets task ids from `list_todoist_tasks` and must not read them aloud.</li><li>**Lookup before the card:** the server looks the task up first (`TODOIST_GET_TASK2`) and builds the card from what Todoist returned, not the agent's words. A missing or already-done task is refused before any approval exists.</li><li>**Approval:** bound to the arguments plus that task. The task is looked up again right before the write.</li><li>**Results:** "completed" only on Composio's successful answer. "moved" only with the due Todoist sent back; otherwise the result is "unknown".</li><li>**Guard:** allows exactly these two Todoist write slugs, only in the catalog and its mock.</li></ul> | Backend mock tests (152 pass, 19 new). Slugs and shapes were read from Composio's schemas on 2026-10-11, but nothing has run against a real Todoist account. |
| 304 | **Read one whole email, when asked.** <ul><li>**Tool:** `read_email` (`query`) reads only the newest match: sender, subject, received time and the text.</li><li>**Text handling:** cut to 1,500 characters, with control, invisible and bidi characters, HTML tags and links removed. It is framed to the agent as information, never instructions.</li><li>**Switch:** its own switch, "Read an email", off until the user turns it on.</li><li>**Receipt:** no subject, sender or text.</li><li>The privacy paragraph says that this one email goes to ElevenLabs during that conversation.</li></ul> | Backend mock tests. A real Gmail account NOT RUN. |
| 305 | **A dish's ingredients to a list.** <ul><li>**Tool:** `add_ingredients` (`list`, `dish`, `items`, at most 30, repeats dropped). The items are Doddy's suggestions, and the card and reply say so.</li><li>**Card:** every item has a tick. Anything already open on the list starts unticked and is marked. Only ticked items are added, through the same add as `add_to_list`, read back, and can be undone from What Doddy did.</li><li>Recipe or web text alone is never a request.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`IngredientsTests`). |
| 306 | **Weekly look-back.** <ul><li>Off by default.</li><li>**Notification:** once a week, on the day and at the time the user picks (Sunday 18:00 to start; 19:00 since ADR-324), it says only "Your week with Doddy".</li><li>**Screen:** built on this iPhone from notes saved in the last 7 days (their own words, up to 5), list items ticked, changes Doddy made and checked (undone ones don't count), and, if Calendar or Reminders are on, the next 7 days' events and reminders due.</li><li>No streaks, scores, comparisons or nudges. Nothing is sent anywhere.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`WeeklyLookBackTests`). Notification NOT RUN. |
| 307 | **A shared list's place reminder for everyone.** <ul><li>**Share:** on a shared list with a place reminder, "Share this place with everyone on the list" writes the picked place (name, point, radius, arrive or leave, and who shared it) to one `DoddyListPlace` record in the list's iCloud zone, then reads it back. Syncing items never touches that record.</li><li>**Others:** they see "Marta shared a reminder at “Lidl”", and "Remind me there too" sets their own place reminder, which iOS watches on their iPhone.</li><li>A place within 50 m, arriving or leaving alike, counts as already set. Whoever shared it can stop sharing.</li><li>Nobody's whereabouts are shared, only the place that was picked. The data-flow row and privacy paragraph say so.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`SharedListPlaceTests`). CloudKit NOT RUN; the `DoddyListPlace` record type needs the CloudKit schema deployed. |
| 313 | **Doddy moves, and shows up outside the app** (owner: "do all" for the two lists proposed after ADR-308; the weather poses and alternate app icons were left out because they need an owner decision first). All art from the owner's pack, cut, cropped or downscaled only (provenance in `docs/design/UPLOADED_MEMORY_ASSETS.md`). **Poses:** new `IslandPose.basketballDribble` (focus) and `.onelegBalance` (breathing); with Whole Doddy the focus and breathing islands show them, paused is cross-legged, done the fist pump (`DoddyToolAttributes.wholeDoddyPose`). **Scenery:** the court strip on the focus screen with Doddy dribbling (cross-legged when paused, fist pump only when the time ran out); the rooftop strip with Doddy cross-legged on the weekly look-back (`SceneryBackdrop` gains `.court`, `.rooftop` and a `pose`). **Strips** (`DoddyMotion`, cut by `cut_frames.py --shared` so every frame keeps the strip's scale and ground): the voice screen walks while connecting and runs while a plan's steps run (the island keeps the stretch; it can't animate); the save receipt plays the pop, then the existing fist pump, only after the save is confirmed; a list's last item ticked here plays the jump once; an undone entry in What Doddy did shows the turned-back frame; Home plays four idle breaths once per launch when the opening (ADR-225) doesn't play. Reduce Motion shows one still frame everywhere. **Outside the app:** the Lock Screen Talk to Doddy widget shows Doddy's face; a new small widget "Doddy, resting" for StandBy (the stretch pose and the next reminder's time, never its words); note reminders carry the reaching pose as a picture (no extra words; none with Hide Doddy's pictures); lists can be shared as a picture (the same open items as the text, drawn only when shared); Lists, What Doddy did and Tidy Up show Doddy cross-legged when empty; Siri's "Ask Doddy what I saved" panel shows the pose for the answer (listening found, shrug none, facepalm failed, shush switched off). **Not done:** the scenery parallax: the pack's foreground layers sit below the part the strips show (ADR-308), so nothing would move. | Linux: island checks PASS with new tool-pose, voice-screen and strip checks and every new image set required (planted bugs caught); `swiftc -parse` on every changed file; Polish catalog and pack integrity PASS; core CI PASS. **iOS, widget, Siri and notification behaviour NOT RUN** (no Xcode); Mac checklist 4i (Y1–Y12). Not tried on a device |

## Round 5 (2026-10-11, owner: "do all of them")
Nine items: ADR-310 to ADR-312 and ADR-314 to ADR-318. ADR-308, ADR-309 and ADR-313 were taken on the default branch while this round was being built (scenery, camera shortcut, Doddy moves). Commit messages on this branch from before the renumbering use 308 to 312 for what is now 310 to 314; the memo to-dos were then 313 and are now 318. Nothing in this round has been compiled with Xcode.

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 310 | **Face ID lock and private notes.** <ul><li>**Lock:** Settings › Privacy › "Lock Doddy with Face ID", off by default. When it is on, Doddy asks for Face ID, or the passcode, when it opens and after a minute away (`LAContext`, `.deviceOwnerAuthentication`). Turning it on or off also needs Face ID. iOS checks the face; Doddy only learns yes or no. A cover hides the whole app until then.</li><li>**Private notes:** a note's "Private" switch. Their ids are kept in UserDefaults and read back after each change. One core filter (`PrivateNotes.shareable`) runs at every way out: the voice agent's notes and search, Siri's note lookup, Ask Doddy, widgets, the watch, the call copy and the weekly look-back. When only a private note matched, the voice agent hears "no match".</li><li>**In the app:** lists and Find show "Private note" instead of the words, and a private note opens on a locked screen with Unlock. Copy and share are hidden until it is unlocked.</li><li>`NSFaceIDUsageDescription` (EN/PL) is added. The guard allows LocalAuthentication only in `Features/Privacy/AppLock.swift`.</li><li>**Backups (owner, 2026-10-11: "should be encrypted"):** private notes stay in the backup file, which is already sealed with the user's passphrase (AES-256-GCM, ADR-222). On the iPhone, Doddy's database uses iOS file protection like every other note. Nothing changed in code.</li><li>**Amended 18 October:** the first upload after switching "Memory on calls" on sent every note, private ones included (the Calls screen passed the unfiltered list). The filter now sits where the upload is built: `MemoryCopyItem.from` requires the private set, and `MemoryCopySync` reads it just before each upload, so no caller can skip it. Owner, 18 October: "force one filtered upload on first launch". Once after the update, Doddy replaces the server copy at launch or when it comes to the front, while the copy is on and only after the notes were read successfully (`MemoryCopyResend`); it counts as done only when the server confirmed an upload.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`PrivateNotesTests`, `CallsClientTests`); app-logic type-check PASS (does not cover `MemoryCopySync`). Face ID NOT RUN. |
| 311 | **"Save to Doddy" in the Share Sheet.** <ul><li>**Extension:** a new share extension, `DoddyShare`, for text and one web link. It shows what was shared and, after **Send to Doddy**, writes one item to the App Group (`share-inbox.json`, complete file protection) and reads it back. ## Round 6 (2026-10-11, owner: "do all of them, skip the camera one")
Nine items. The default branch had reached ADR-323, so this round starts at ADR-330. ADR-331 was then taken on the default branch (hide character art), so quick actions are ADR-339; ADR-336 was taken there too (previews and UI tests), so Outlook is ADR-340. Scanning a document with the camera was left out by the owner (ADR-224 stands). Nothing in this round has been compiled with Xcode.

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 330 | **Notes and lists in iPhone search.** <ul><li>Settings › Privacy › "Show notes in iPhone search", **off by default**.</li><li>When on, Doddy keeps a CoreSpotlight index (its own domain, protected until first unlock) of note text and list names with open items, shortened, tags as keywords. It is rebuilt only when that content changes.</li><li>Private notes (ADR-310) are never indexed. Turning it off removes the whole domain.</li><li>A result opens the note or list, as a widget link does.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`SpotlightIndexTests`). Spotlight NOT RUN. |
| 332 | **"When am I free?"** <ul><li>A phone tool, `find_free_time`: from a day, for up to 7 days, between earliest and latest (default 08:00–20:00), with a shortest useful gap (default 30 min).</li><li>Read-only: it reads the iPhone's calendars and works out the gaps between timed events on the phone. All-day events are named, not counted; today starts now.</li><li>Nothing is booked; the agent is told to use create_calendar_event.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`FreeTimeTests`). EventKit NOT RUN. |
| 333 | **Tags in notes.** <ul><li>A word written with # in a note is its tag ("C#", "a#b" and URL anchors aren't; "#1" isn't). Read from the note's current words, nothing added or guessed.</li><li>Memories shows a chip per tag to filter by. A private note's tags show only after Face ID.</li><li>A voice search for exactly "#tag" returns the notes with that tag, newest first; private notes stay out.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`NoteTagsTests`). |
| 334 | **List templates.** <ul><li>"Save as template" in a list's menu keeps its name and items as written (ticks not kept); saving again under the same name replaces it. At most 20, kept in the app's preferences on this iPhone and read back.</li><li>New list › "Start from a template" fills the name and items. By voice, `create_list` takes `template`; the template's items come first, and the usual card shows everything before anything is made.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`ListTemplatesTests`). |
| 335 | **"Remind me when to leave."** <ul><li>A phone tool, `remind_when_to_leave` (`place`, `arrive_by`, `mode` driving/walking/transit, `buffer_minutes` 0–60, default 10).</li><li>The app reads where the iPhone is once (While Using; approximate is enough), searches Apple Maps near it, and asks Apple Maps for the travel time to arrive by then (MapKit only in Connections). The card shows the place Maps found, the travel time and when to leave.</li><li>Only **Set reminder** schedules a local notification (English or Polish), read back from iOS. Too late or over 12 hours is refused before any card.</li><li>The position and the place go to Apple once; neither is kept. The location prompt (EN/PL), privacy paragraph and data-flow row say so.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`LeaveByTests`). MapKit and notifications NOT RUN. |
| 337 | **iPad.** <ul><li>The app, widget extension and share extension target iPhone and iPad; every iPad orientation, so Split View and Slide Over work.</li><li>On a wide screen the tabs keep one readable column (760 pt).</li><li>Back Tap setup, an iPhone-only setting, is hidden on iPad.</li></ul> | NOT RUN (no Xcode, no iPad). |
| 338 | **Rules for Doddy in the car** (groundwork for CarPlay, ADR-317). <ul><li>`CarProfile.decision` covers every voice request: look-ups run; saving a note and adding list items wait for a spoken yes to an exact read-back; everything else that changes something, or leaves the iPhone, is refused while driving.</li><li>Only a short plain yes counts ("yes", "yes please", "tak", "zapisz"…); "yes but…" is a no.</li><li>No CarPlay screen yet; that waits for Apple's entitlement.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`CarProfileTests`). |
| 339 | **Quick actions on the app icon.** <ul><li>Long-press: Talk to Doddy, New note, Find in Doddy, and Where I parked when a spot is saved.</li><li>Each only opens a screen through the same router as the shortcuts; the parking one hands the spot to Maps.</li><li>The menu is refreshed when Doddy goes to the background.</li></ul> | Linux tests (`QuickActionTests`). NOT RUN on a device. |
| 340 | **Outlook (Microsoft 365 and Outlook.com).** <ul><li>A new online-account toolkit with four tools, each with its own switch, off: `check_outlook_email` and `read_outlook_email` (`OUTLOOK_LIST_MESSAGES`, inbox only, text cleaned and framed as information), `draft_outlook_email` (`OUTLOOK_CREATE_DRAFT`, a write behind a card, never sent; no id or `isDraft:false` is "unknown"), and `read_outlook_calendar` (`OUTLOOK_GET_CALENDAR_VIEW`, read-only, up to 31 days, cancelled events left out).</li><li>Grants: Mail.Read / Mail.ReadWrite / Calendars.Read (with User.Read, offline_access); anything wider, such as Mail.Send, blocks every Outlook tool.</li><li>The capability guard now checks Outlook slugs: only `OUTLOOK_CREATE_DRAFT` may write, only in the catalog and its mock.</li></ul> | Backend mock tests (188 pass, 20 new). Slugs read from Composio's schemas on 2026-10-11. Not run against a real Microsoft account. |
| 380 | **The whole wave and four storyboards** (owner: "do all" on "what else we could add"). (1) The pack's wave (016) now plays as a strip of five frames, `DoddyMotion.wave`: Home waves once the first time it shows before noon on a day (`MorningWave`, the day kept on this iPhone only, tested in core), otherwise breathes and sits as before (ADR-323); the morning check-in has a small Doddy wave over the coffee strip; the "Doddy_waving" hello on the note-reminder explainer plays it too. (2) `PanelStory` and `PanelStoryView` play any of the pack's storyboards as cross-fades (the welcome's page turn, ADR-299, now uses it): the phone storyboard (037) introduces the Back Tap guide, the ink storyboard (040) opens "What I can do", the door storyboard (039) is on the Face ID cover (ADR-310): the locked cover shows him drawing the door; only after iOS says yes the door opens and he walks through for 0.8 s, a tap skips it, and a cancelled or failed check never opens it. (3) The keys storyboard (041) is a six-picture worked example under "What I can do", captioned in English and Polish, clearly an example and saying Doddy only finds what the user told it. (4) Reduce Motion shows the last panel or frame at once; Hide character artwork shows no pictures (the keys example becomes six lines of text, the lock keeps the padlock). (5) The five foreground layers (024 to 035) are not used: a layer in front of Doddy would tint him and sit beside text that ADR-308 keeps clean, and it can only be judged on a device. The umbrella pose waits for the weather decision. (6) Provenance and hashes in `docs/design/UPLOADED_MEMORY_ASSETS.md`; `tools/whole_doddy/cut_storyboards.py` and `build_380` cut and build the sets; `check_island_art.sh` now checks every storyboard panel and wave frame exists. | Linux: `swiftc -parse` on every changed file; `MorningWaveTests` (4); core CI and island checks PASS. **App, previews and UI NOT RUN**; Mac checklist 4m |
| 381 | **Every reply that carried outside text now marks it as data** (owner: "do all"; follows review C4, ADR-380's audit). About 40 reply builders still interpolated text the user, the voice model or another app had written into the first line the agent reads: event, timer, alarm and reminder titles, place and contact searches, list and template names, parking notes, tag lists, free-time event titles, "not found" echoes, clarification problems, and the whole of an online account's answer (its first line holds Todoist titles, draft recipients and search queries). Each keeps its fixed result prefix (`added:`, `opened:`, `failed:`, `not found:`, …) so the activity log, plan summary and agent rules read it as before, and the text moves to a `ToolData.block` after it (`VoiceToolReply.quoted`). Online-account answers are data as a whole, with a known result word in front; read results get no prefix. The agent's data rule in `voice-agent/instructions.md` now says data includes words it passed to a tool and the server's answers (**re-apply it in ElevenLabs**). A cleaner long-term fix is server-side: a first line with no data. `ReplyDataMarkingTests` pass a hostile string through about 45 builders and check the first line is unchanged and the string appears only inside the markers. Left alone, each with its reason in the commit: replies built only from fixed app text, ECB codes, unit symbols, app-formatted clocks. | Linux: core CI PASS (865 tests, 4 new); voice-agent spec check PASS. **Not run:** the app (`VoiceSessionController` is not in the type-check list) and a live conversation; Mac checklist M9 |

## OpenAI Realtime is the default voice (2026-10-13, owner: "we're only gonna be changing the model to realtime as it is cheaper"; "a switcher inside the app between realtime 2.1 and 2.1 mini")

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 442 | **OpenAI Realtime runs Doddy's voice by default; ElevenLabs stays as a switch; Home picks `gpt-realtime-2.1` or `gpt-realtime-2.1-mini`.** Architecture unchanged: same prompt, tools, cards and on-device data (ADR-265). <ul><li>**Default rule:** a stored choice wins; with none, OpenAI where the token server is set, else ElevenLabs (it cannot start OpenAI without the server). `VoiceProvider.effective`.</li><li>**Model:** default mini (cheaper). The app names the model in the token request; the server allowlists exactly those two ids and refuses others, so the client cannot pick a pricier model. An older server ignores the field and uses `OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL`.</li><li>**Honest copy:** privacy strings, the microphone prompt (English + Polish), the site and the data-flow rows no longer say conversations go to ElevenLabs when OpenAI is running them. OpenAI's retention of Realtime audio is unverified and stays stated as such.</li><li>**Kept out:** ElevenLabs removal, new tools for weather/Wikipedia on OpenAI, any change to calls or Hey Doddy.</li></ul> | Token server and catalog/spec checks pass on Linux (20 token-server tests). **Swift tests and app build NOT RUN (no toolchain here).** Owner: set `OPENAI_API_KEY` and a spending limit on the OpenAI project; judge mini in Polish and with tools on a device; confirm consent wording for a default that sends voice to OpenAI. `docs/implementation/VOICE_PROVIDER_TEST_STATUS.md` |

## Learning loop (2026-10-11)
`prompts/LEARNING_LOOP.md`: ADR-440 is the Phase A design and Phase B storage; ADR-441 is Phase C, the app wiring. Design: `docs/architecture/LEARNING_LOOP.md`.

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 440 | **Learning is data, never code.** Doddy gets better for its one user through four stores, all local SQLite: verified facts (proposed, then confirmed word for word), procedures (saved plan templates over the ADR-204 allowlist, run through the same plan card and per-step approvals), outcome events (kinds only, no user text) and capability gaps (categories only). <ul><li>**Why data, not code:** nothing on the iPhone can review, test or roll back generated code; a learned row can be shown, edited, retired and deleted. New capabilities keep arriving through reviewed pull requests; the gap counts tell the owner which to build.</li><li>**On-device self-modification rejected**; so are implicit learning (DOD-06), model-decided risk tiers, embeddings and any cloud store.</li><li>**Procedures compose only catalog tools:** each step names a tool in `voice-agent/tools.json` that `PlanTool.allowed` lists, whose connection is on; look-ups and app-leaving tools are never steps.</li><li>**Approval tiers are preserved:** the tier is derived from the registry at run time and not stored, so no row can lower it or skip a card; a fact whose text reads like an instruction is grounding text only.</li><li>**No silent learning:** every item is confirmed on a card bound to the exact text; the same evidence digest is never proposed twice; "Saved" only after read-back.</li><li>Schema proposed as migrations `v11_learned_facts`, `v12_procedures`, `v13_outcome_events` (design doc §8), appended after `v10`; nothing shipped is edited.</li><li>Milestone M6, after M2 (real model) and M4 (real actions), placed after the M5 decision so it does not confound the beta. Metric: user corrections per week, local, with a two-week baseline first.</li></ul> | **Owner accepted the six defaults (2026-10-11). Phase B built:** typed core, migrations `v11`–`v13`, three SQLite repositories, 31 Linux tests PASS (Swift 6.1, real SQLite). **Phase C not built**; nothing on a screen; iOS NOT RUN. `docs/implementation/M6_LEARNING_LOOP_STATUS.md`. |
| 441 | **Learning loop, Phase C: how it is wired into the app** (owner, 2026-10-11: "start Phase C"). <ul><li>**Off until turned on:** "Learn from how I use Doddy" and "Use what Doddy learned in voice conversations" both start off. Counting corrections runs either way (tool names and kinds only, 90 days, never sent) so the two-week baseline exists before any claim that Doddy gets better.</li><li>**Facts come from the owner's own words** through the ADR-247 rules, from sentences the "Worth keeping?" card did not already offer. The evidence is the folded sentence, so "Never this" holds across conversations.</li><li>**Routines come from repeated tools:** the same ordered 2–5 plan-allowlist tools completed in one conversation on 3 separate days within 30 days. No parameter values are ever recorded, so every parameter is asked at run time. A sequence that has any row (refused, retired) is never proposed again on new evidence.</li><li>**At most one card per conversation**, 3 a day, 7-day expiry, none for private conversations, none while connections are paused, nothing matching the never-learned list.</li><li>**Running a routine is by voice only:** the agent is handed the confirmed routines, asks for the details, and calls `propose_plan`; the existing plan card and per-step approvals run it. A routine used this way is counted when a plan with exactly its tools ran. There is no "Run" button in Settings, because the plan card lives in the voice session.</li><li>**Capability gaps** use a new agent tool `note_capability_gap` with an enumerated category and no free text. It changes nothing, uses no app and leaves no row in What Doddy did. The live ElevenLabs agent does not have it until the owner syncs it.</li><li>**Not built:** the typed Ask screen does not use learned facts (the gateway limits would have to be raised first); no sensitive-mode router exists, so that flag is always off and only the content filter applies; gaps are not detected by rule when the agent does not call the tool.</li></ul> | **Built and Linux-tested** (core, service and SQLite tests; type-check of the store). **Screens, voice wiring and live agent: NOT RUN.** Mac checklist 4k (L1–L13). |

Numbered 324 because 300–323 are taken or reserved on other branches (first drafted as 310, then 319). The defaults of Settings themselves are unchanged: all of these stay off for anyone who skips the screen. `docs/implementation/QUICK_START_STATUS.md`. | Linux: `QuickStartTests` (9) PASS; `scripts/ci.sh --core-only` PASS; `swiftc -parse` on every changed Swift file. **iOS build and UI tests NOT RUN** (no Xcode); Mac checklist 4r (Q1–Q5). ## First-use fixes from the onboarding review (2026-10-11)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 325 | **Two fixes from the onboarding review** (owner: "zrób 1 i 2 w osobnym PR", after Claude walked the path from install to the first conversation; first drafted as ADR-311, then ADR-320; renumbered because 310–323 are taken or reserved on other branches). (1) **The ElevenLabs / OpenAI test switch (ADR-265) moves from Home to Settings › Voice.** On Home it sat right under Talk to Doddy for every new user, and nobody new can know what it means. It keeps working the same, including in TestFlight builds, so the comparison the owner asked for is still possible; only where it lives changes. (2) **Listening on open waits for the microphone to be allowed** (changes ADR-225's rule). Before, only a *refused* microphone stopped it, so a user who hadn't talked on day one got iOS's microphone question the moment they next opened Doddy, 2.3 s after launch and without having asked for anything. Now `OpeningConditions.microphoneAllowed` must be true: "not asked yet" counts as no, the first microphone question always follows a tap on Talk to Doddy, and from then on listening on open works as before. The switch stays on by default and its Settings note says so. | Linux: `OpeningPolicyTests` PASS (new test for "not yet asked"); `scripts/ci.sh --core-only` PASS (750 tests); `swiftc -parse` on every changed Swift file. **iOS build and UI tests NOT RUN** (no Xcode; Actions runners not starting); Mac checklist 4o (O1–O2). ## Voice tools, privacy and test fixes from the DODDY Brain audit (2026-10-12 to 2026-10-13)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 448 | **UI tests play the opening on a slower clock** (flake on Bienos/doddy.2#64 and #65, 2026-10-13). `testOpeningEndsByItselfAndIsNotRepeatedOnAQuickReturn` failed at its first check on two CI runs while the same code passed on others. The opening's whole timeline is 2.4 s, and on the CI simulator the launch plus XCUITest's first idle wait took about 5.9 s before the test looked, so the scene had sometimes already ended. <ul><li>**Now:** a launch argument `-openingTimeScale` slows the opening's clock (`OpeningScene.sceneTime`, held between 1× and 10×). `UITestLaunch` passes 6× whenever a test turns the opening on, so it lasts 14.4 s, is on screen when the test first looks, and still ends by itself. A tap still skips to the hand-over.</li><li>**Not a setting:** nothing in the app writes the key; on a phone the opening plays at real speed. The timeline itself is unchanged and stays covered by `OpeningSceneTests`.</li><li>**Not skipped or weakened:** both opening tests keep every assertion; only their wait timeouts grow with the slower clock.</li></ul> |
| 446 | **What Doddy did keeps a note's words only as long as Undo needs them** (DODDY Brain audit 2026-10-12 §5 #7; DoddyCore review C2 follow-up). C2 had already removed note text and searches from the rows themselves (`logDetail`). Two copies were left: **Undo's data** (`ActivityUndo.savedNote(text:)`, list items) stayed with the row for its full 30 days although Undo is offered for one day, and **Delete all memories** didn't touch the log. <ul><li>**After a day:** `ActivityLog.pruned` drops a row's Undo data once Undo can no longer be used (`ActivityUndo.window`). The row, its outcome and any Undo result stay. The app prunes at launch and on every new row, and saves.</li><li>**Deleting a note or list:** `MemoryStore.delete` calls `ActivityLogStore.forget(memoryID:)`. Rows that could undo that note or list lose their Undo data and keep the rest.</li><li>**Delete all memories:** `MemoryStore.deleteAll` calls `forgetNotes()`. The Notes and lists rows go; Calendar, Reminders and other apps' rows stay, since those changes still exist. The confirmation now says so (EN + PL).</li><li>**Trade-off:** a note deleted and then brought back with the Undo bar no longer offers Undo of its voice save in What Doddy did.</li></ul> | **Core built and Linux-tested** (DoddyCore 926 pass, 6 in `ActivityRetentionTests`; 3 shown failing on the old code). `MemoryStore`/`ActivityLogStore` type-checked on Linux. **`DoddyApp.swift`, `SettingsView.swift`: app build NOT RUN.** Mac checklist 4y. |
| 447 | **Settings → Privacy's Network and Cloud AI rows come from What leaves this iPhone** (DODDY Brain audit 2026-10-12 §5 #6). Both were fixed strings that had fallen behind the per-service screen and the paragraph under them. Network named only ElevenLabs, the token server, Connected Apps and Ask Doddy, missing the messaging server, Apple Maps, the ECB rates file, iCloud list sharing, Siri, Picovoice and Open-Meteo/Wikipedia. Cloud AI missed the OpenAI voice and the messaging server's calls and news writer. <ul><li>**Now:** `DataFlow.networkSummary` and `cloudAISummary` are built from `DataFlow.rows` for the settings right now, so a service that's off isn't named and one that's on can't be left out.</li><li>**Drift guard:** every row id has a Network name or is listed as deliberately left out (`translator` is ElevenLabs; `backup` is a file you save). `DataFlowSummaryTests` fails when a new row is added without one.</li><li>**One source of settings:** `DataFlowState.current()` reads the switches once, for both screens, using their @AppStorage defaults. Some start on, which `UserDefaults.bool` would have read as off.</li><li>**Not changed:** the paragraph under the rows, and the rows' language. Like What leaves this iPhone, they are English only; the old row text was too.</li><li>**With ADR-442** (OpenAI Realtime the default voice where the token server is set): both rows name whichever service runs the voice, OpenAI or ElevenLabs, because they follow the rows' active state; `DataFlowState.current()` uses `VoiceProvider.effective` like the voice controller.</li></ul> | **Core built and Linux-tested** (`DataFlowSummaryTests`, 4). The new `DataFlowState.current()` was type-checked on Linux against DoddyCore with stand-ins; `DataFlowView.swift` and `SettingsView.swift` **app build NOT RUN** here (CI on the Mac). Mac checklist 4w. |
| 445 | **A sign-in that isn't completed through Doddy never leaves a live grant** (DODDY Brain audit 2026-10-12 §5 #4; backend review follow-up). Finding 2 (`ad7db03`) removed connections when a completion failed after the attempt was consumed. Still open were: a **superseded** attempt (a second Connect), a **late** completion of an expired attempt, and an **abandoned** one (consent finished in the browser, app never returned). In the last case `prune` deleted the attempt and the only copy of its connection id a day later. <ul><li>**Rule:** ending an attempt without redeeming it queues its pending connection for removal in the same transaction (`Store.endAttempts`). That covers a supersede, expiry, and a link that comes back after its attempt was superseded. A connection recorded in `connections` is never queued. Every pending id was made by Doddy for this user's own Composio id, so removing it can't touch anyone else's.</li><li>**When it is removed:** Connect removes the superseded one at once; a late `complete` removes the expired one at once. An hourly `sweepAbandoned` in both services (run before `prune` in `app.ts`) ends expired attempts and retries failed removals: older than 5 min, 20 a sweep, 24 tries per row. After that the row stays `failed`, visible, and is retried before the next Connect.</li><li>**Not changed:** the callback-mode `connectedAccountId` hint is still never removed (unverified). No schema change. No erase-account route (owner decision, review finding 5).</li></ul> | **Built and tested** (backend `npm test` 195 pass; 7 new in `abandoned-attempts.test.ts`, 2 of them shown failing on the old code). **Live Composio: NOT RUN.** |
| 458 | **A tool call belongs to the conversation it arrived in** (stale-session guard; DODDY Brain audit 2026-10-12, §5 #1). Before, `handle` checked `isLive` only on entry, `propose` checked `isLive` and `reply` sent to whatever conversation was current, so a slow lookup (Gmail/Drive `prepare`, recall, Maps, location) that finished after End and a new Start could show conversation A's card in conversation B, where Confirm ran a real write, and B's agent got A's result. <ul><li>**Rule:** each call is recorded with the controller's start number when it arrives (`ToolCallOwners`, DoddyCore). Its card is shown, its screen state set (list receipt, parking card, place choice, plan ticks, connection prompt) and its result sent only while that number is still current. Every start and every End changes the number. A call never recorded counts as stale, so a missed path fails closed.</li><li>**Where:** at the two places every path ends (`propose`, `reply`), plus each place that changes the screen or a list after an `await` (`handlePlan` after its checks, note reminders after the Maps search, ingredients, bulk, parking, the list tools, `notConnected`). OpenAI calls capture the number before their `Task` starts.</li><li>**What a dropped call does:** nothing is shown or sent; what it asked is logged in What Doddy did as not done (once: `stop()` usually logged it already). A change the user already confirmed keeps running and its real result is still logged; only the send to the new conversation is skipped, and a plan's progress shows only on its own screen.</li><li>**`ToolGate.decide`** reproduces the checks before the tool switch (translator, plan, Pause all, the tool's connection, in that order) as a pure function; the controller now calls it. Behaviour is unchanged.</li><li>**Confirmed changes:** the start number is read at the tap and passed down (plan steps included); after an `await` only that conversation's screen shows the outcome (reminder and note-reminder status, list receipts, the saved count and island flash, connection prompts, the Undo receipt).</li><li>**List tools by voice** (add, tick, remove) get an "in progress" row before they write, as confirmed cards do, so End mid-write leaves "in progress" (then "Not sure" after a crash) until the real result replaces it, instead of a wrong "not done". The learning loop records the same "completed" event either way.</li></ul> | **Core built and Linux-tested** (`ToolGateTests`, 9 tests; full DoddyCore suite 929 pass). **Controller edit: app build NOT RUN** (no Xcode; the controller is not in `scripts/typecheck_app_logic.sh`). Mac checklist 4x (S1–S8). |
| 444 | **One tool catalog, checked against the agent's spec and the server** (DODDY Brain Phase 3b). `ToolCatalog` in DoddyCore has one `ToolDescriptor` per tool in `voice-agent/tools.json` (58: 38 phone, 16 online-account, 4 web). Only two things are declared there, for phone and web tools: the **effect** (lookup, change on the iPhone, account write, hand-off to another app, the conversation itself) and the **approval** (none, card, card plus the server's single-use approval). Everything else is read from the constants that already decide it: the switch (`VoiceConnection.serving`), plan use (`PlanTool.allowed`), the online-account tools and their read/write (`CloudToolName`). <ul><li>**`ToolCatalogTests` (13)** fail when: a tool is in the spec and not the app, or the reverse; `runs_on` differs; `confirm_on_screen` differs from "approval is not none"; a card tool's timeout is under 60 s; a phone tool isn't parsed by `VoiceToolRequest`; a lookup asks for a tap; an account write lacks the server approval; a hand-off has no card; a change without a card is added outside the listed ones (list add/tick/remove, the capability-gap row, stop translator); a plan step isn't a change; a switch serves no tool; or the server's `CLOUD_TOOLS` read/write (`backend/src/tools/catalog.ts`) differs from `CloudToolName`. Checked by breaking the catalog, the spec and the server file on purpose: each break failed the right tests.</li><li>**Not changed:** no app code reads the catalog yet, so no behaviour changes; the controller's switch still decides what runs. The design's risk tiers (L0–L4) and `CapabilityResolver` are not built: assigning tiers to deletes and hand-offs is an owner decision.</li><li>**Found:** `parking_spot` shows a card only for "save"; "clear" removes the saved spot without one (ADR-301). The catalog records the card; whether "clear" should ask is an owner question. `PlanTool.allowed` includes `add_to_list`, which has no card of its own: the plan card is its approval, so the design's "plan tools ⊆ card tools" rule doesn't hold and isn't tested.</li></ul> | **Built and Linux-tested** (DoddyCore 942 tests pass, 13 in `ToolCatalogTests`). No app or agent change; nothing to run on a Mac. |
| 449 | **The stale-session guard is tested in the app, through the real controller** (DODDY Brain audit 2026-10-12 §5 #10; follow-up to ADR-458). ADR-458's rules were tested in DoddyCore (`ToolGateTests`), but nothing drove `VoiceSessionController` itself through End and Start. <ul><li>**Seams (DEBUG only):** `beginForTesting(link:)` makes a conversation live as `start` does (a new start number, a stand-in provider, no microphone). `receiveToolCallForTesting` delivers a tool call tagged with the start it arrived in, as the ElevenLabs and OpenAI callbacks do. The app never calls either.</li><li>**`StaleToolCallTests` (7):** a tool call's Reminders or notification lookup is held, End and Start are tapped, then the lookup finishes. Checked: no card in the new conversation, no reply to either conversation, and nothing written. Also: a call still queued at End reads nothing; a card on screen at End runs nothing after the next Start. Two control tests run the same held calls without End: the card shows, the approved delete runs once, and the "not found" answer reaches the conversation that asked. A guard that dropped everything would fail those two.</li><li>The harness turns on the Calendar, Reminders and Timers switches and puts them back, with the "had a first conversation" flag (ADR-326) that reaching Listening sets, so the app's UI tests still see a first visit.</li><li>Covers the Mac checklist item "End + Start while a Gmail draft is preparing" for Reminders and Timers. Cloud tools (Gmail) go through the same `propose` and `reply` checks but need a fake server, so they stay on the checklist.</li></ul> |

## Action status model (2026-10-11, owner: "start the action status model"; first numbered ADR-290, then 291–293, then 300–303, then 309–312 on this branch; the default branch took those, so these are 319–322)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 319 | **Action status model: typed status, receipts, status history, and a retry that checks first** (OP-012 + OP-013 from the research backlog; owner: "start the action status model"; `docs/implementation/ACTION_STATUS_STATUS.md`). Every change in "What Doddy did" now has a typed `ActionStatus`: proposed, pending, confirmed, failed, unknown, declined, or handed off. Only these moves are allowed (`ActionStatus.canMove`): proposed → pending, declined or failed; pending → confirmed, failed, unknown or handed off; unknown → confirmed or failed, and only through a check of the app itself. Confirmed, failed, declined and handed off are final. "Check in Reminders" now changes only an unsure entry; before, it would rewrite any entry it was given. Each entry keeps its **status history** (in progress 10:02 → not sure 10:02 → confirmed 10:05), and a **receipt** on confirmed entries only: what Reminders or Calendar read back (title, time, list) and the app's own identifier, kept on this iPhone and never sent to the agent. **Retry:** new reminders and new events carry an operation key: the same change asked again has the same key. Before the card is shown, `ActionRetry` reads the log. If an earlier try is unconfirmed or still running, the add looks in the app first, never adds a second copy, and the reply says "Doddy checked … first because an earlier try wasn't confirmed". After 3 tries in a day where the app was asked to write and nothing was confirmed, Doddy stops, shows no card, and asks the user to look themselves. A plan step shows "Tried 3 times …" instead. Clarifications, a switched-off connection, or a "no" don't count as tries. **Calendar now looks before it writes** (`EventAddAction`), as Reminders already did (ADR-126): an event with the same title, start and end (the same day for all-day events) counts as already there. A failed save is looked for again before Doddy answers, and "added" comes only from a read-back that matches the card. An unsure reminder or event now tells the agent that asking again is safe, because Doddy checks first. Timers and alarms (ADR-320), notes and lists (ADR-321) and online accounts (ADR-322) were added later. iOS has no public link to open one specific reminder, so the receipt is shown, not opened. | Core + Linux tests (23 new); app wiring parse-checked only; not compiled with Xcode |
| 320 | **Timers and alarms join the action status model** (owner: "extend it to timers and alarms"; extends ADR-319; `docs/implementation/ACTION_STATUS_STATUS.md`). New timers and alarms now get an operation key, a receipt, a status history and the retry rule, like reminders and events.<br>**Alarms:** the same alarm twice is never wanted, so Doddy always looks first. One of Doddy's alarms with the same time, day or repeat, and label counts as already set (alarms set before this, which have no key, are matched by time, day and label). Doddy notes the alarm (time, label, key) before asking AlarmKit to set it under an id Doddy chose. "Set" only when AlarmKit lists that id. After an error, a listed id still counts as set; otherwise the note is dropped. A note AlarmKit doesn't list yet is kept for 10 minutes, so an alarm listed late is still found on a retry.<br>**Timers:** two identical timers can be wanted on purpose, so Doddy looks for a running copy only when an earlier try of the same timer is unconfirmed, and only for one started since that try. The timer's key goes in its notification identifier, and its ring time in the notification, so the receipt shows the time iOS has. "Started" only when iOS lists the notification. A failed schedule is looked for again before answering.<br>An unsure timer or alarm now tells the agent a retry is safe because Doddy checks first. After 3 unconfirmed tries in a day, Doddy stops and asks the user to look, as for reminders. Not covered: turning an alarm off (already safe to repeat: an alarm that is gone is reported as not found), and timers set before this (no key). | Core + Linux tests (15 new); app wiring parse-checked, `AlarmScheduler` outside AlarmKit type-checked; not compiled with Xcode |
| 321 | **Notes and lists join the action status model** (owner: "extend it to notes and lists"; extends ADR-319/320; `docs/implementation/ACTION_STATUS_STATUS.md`). Notes and lists are in Doddy's own database, which is the truth about them, so Doddy settles an unsure entry itself instead of asking the user to check.<br>Each note or list change keeps a small check with its entry: the note's key (a hash of its words, by DuplicateNote's rule), or the list name and items. `LocalCheck` looks there: a note with those words saved since the try, the items on the list, the items ticked, the items gone, or the list there. Found means confirmed; not found means it didn't happen. A remove from a list that is gone stays unsure, because Doddy can't tell. Settling runs before any retry is decided and when What Doddy did opens, only once the notes have loaded.<br>List changes made without a card (add, tick, remove) are now written down "in progress" before they run, like card changes, so one cut off mid-way shows as "Not sure" and is then settled. If a note save is still unsure at the tap, the note isn't saved twice: one with the same words saved since that try counts as already saved. The same words saved twice on purpose are still two notes.<br>Receipts: the note as the database returned it, and the list's own result (added, already on the list, ticked, removed). Plan steps that save a note or add to a list get the same. Not covered: notes and list items changed from the app's own screens (not logged in What Doddy did), and the remind-about-a-note card. | Core + Linux tests (9 new); app wiring parse-checked, `ActivityLogStore` type-checked; not compiled with Xcode |
| 322 | **Online accounts join the action status model** (owner: "extend it to online accounts"; extends ADR-319 to ADR-321; changes ADR-098's "a used approval is refused"; `docs/implementation/ACTION_STATUS_STATUS.md`, `docs/integrations/ONLINE_ACCOUNTS.md`).<br>**Server:** an approval still runs at most once, and its outcome is now recorded on it: succeeded with the provider's id (Gmail draft id, Todoist task id), may-or-may-not, or failed. The email or task text is not stored. A repeated run with an approval that succeeded gets the first answer (`replayed`) and nothing runs. A repeat of one whose first run may have gone through, or hasn't finished, says so (`earlier_outcome_unknown`). Before, both came back as "the confirmation expired", even when the draft or task had been made. A repeat of one that provably didn't run needs a fresh confirmation, as before. Prepare tells the card when the same write ran in the last day ("Doddy already did exactly this once", or "may have gone through, check first"). After a try whose outcome is unknown, a Todoist task is looked for with one task search, and only when "List my tasks" is switched on. Found means the app answers "already there" with no card. Not listed proves nothing. Gmail drafts can't be looked for, so the card only warns.<br>**Phone:** a confirmed write whose answer is lost on the way (no connection, an unreadable answer) is asked for once more with the same confirmation, which the server can't run twice. Online writes get an operation key (a hash of the tool and its arguments), the 3-try stop, and a receipt with the provider's id, which the agent never hears. Plan steps get the same. Not covered: verification against real Gmail and Todoist accounts (the Composio live path is still unverified, as before). | Backend: 143 tests (10 new) on Node; Core + Linux tests (7 new); app wiring parse-checked; not compiled with Xcode; not run against live Composio |
| 363 | **Round 6's writes join the action status model** (owner: "go"; extends ADR-319 to ADR-322 to ADR-316's Gmail reply draft, ADR-340's Outlook draft and ADR-335's leave reminder; `docs/implementation/ACTION_STATUS_STATUS.md`).<ul><li>**Drafts:** the server now keeps the Outlook and Gmail reply draft ids and answers a repeat of a used confirmation from the first run (`replayed`), instead of "may or may not have gone through". The reply draft's repeated answer uses none of the request's words.</li><li>**Leave reminders:** keyed by place, arrival minute and way of travel. Doddy always looks for the same one first (as for alarms), schedules under an identifier that carries the key, and says "set" only when iOS lists it; a failed add is looked for before answering. The row keeps a receipt (when it shows, place, arrival). No 3-try stop for these: it would ask the user to look for something iOS shows nowhere, and the look-first already prevents a second one.</li></ul> | Core + Linux tests (5 new); backend 198 tests (2 changed); app wiring parse-checked; not compiled with Xcode; not run against live Outlook/Gmail; Mac checklist AS16–AS18 |

## Voice session recovery (2026-10-11, owner: "start OP-023" from the research backlog; numbered 360 (first 330; the default branch then took 330 to 340) to leave room, since parallel branches keep taking the next free numbers)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 360 | **A voice conversation that drops says so, offers what Doddy last heard as a draft, and the next one knows it was cut off** (OP-023, voice fallback and session recovery; `docs/implementation/SESSION_RECOVERY_STATUS.md`).<br>**How it ended** is now typed (`VoiceSessionEnd`):<ul><li>the user ended it;</li><li>Doddy ended it (its end_call);</li><li>a "Hey Doddy" start that nobody spoke into timed out;</li><li>it dropped (offline, the connection closed from the other side, or a voice-service error).</li></ul>The ElevenLabs disconnect reason, which was ignored before, tells these apart. In SDK 3.3.1, `.agent` means the other side closed the connection; `.user` is the SDK's own end, which End and the agent's end_call both use. A drop after Doddy was listening shows **"The conversation dropped"**: "It stopped before you ended it, so Doddy didn't hear anything after that." Before, it showed "Couldn't connect", or looked like a normal "Conversation ended".<br>**Draft, not a note:** after a drop, the user's lines after Doddy's last reply, or the "Save this note?" card that was showing, are offered as an editable draft. It becomes a note only on **Save as note** (read back); **Discard** drops it. It is held on the screen only, never written to disk. Offline, the draft prefills the offline note card. Words still being spoken when it dropped never arrived as text and can't be recovered (the SDK drops tentative transcripts).<br>**Every ending is recorded:** a failure now ends the voice session (the microphone isn't held), and keeps the transcript and the "ended before you answered" rows at once. Before, these waited for Close, and **Talk again** wiped them. The kept conversation log records how it ended (`ended`).<br>**Carrying on:** within 15 minutes, the next conversation's recent-conversations text starts with a note that the newest conversation was cut off. Doddy then offers to carry on and doesn't assume it heard the rest. This follows "Remember recent conversations" (never for Private), and is only added when that conversation is among those sent. No agent prompt change.<br>"Worth keeping?" (ADR-247) is also offered after a drop, without what the draft already offers.<br>Not covered: reconnecting the same session automatically, partial audio, and a paused state for phone calls or Siri (ADR-207's follow-up). | Core + Linux tests (8 new); app wiring parse-checked; not compiled with Xcode; not run on a device |

## Listening privacy (2026-10-11, owner: "do the Polish pass then OP-024"; numbered 361 (first 335), as above)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 361 | **One listening indicator above every screen, and a Stop listening that says "Stopped" only when the microphone is closed** (OP-024, audio privacy signal and stop; owner: "do the Polish pass then OP-024"; `docs/implementation/LISTENING_PRIVACY_STATUS.md`).<ul><li>**Who has the microphone** is read from each user's own state: the conversation (connecting, listening, muted), "Hey Doddy" (its state and Porcupine's recorder) and a voice memo (AVAudioRecorder).</li><li>**Indicator:** a small pill in its own window above every screen and sheet, only while something has the microphone open, saying which ("Listening for “Hey Doddy”", "Recording a voice memo", "Doddy is listening", "Microphone muted"). Muted never says off: the OpenAI test voice keeps capturing while muted. The voice screen keeps its own status instead.</li><li>**Stop listening** (the pill, or Siri "Stop listening in Doddy", allowed while locked): turns "Hey Doddy" off (the switch, not a pause), ends the conversation and stops a memo, reads each one again, then deactivates the audio session. "Stopped" only when each says closed and iOS released the audio; otherwise it names what is still on and offers Try again. Nothing is saved: a "Save this note?" card stays as a draft (ADR-360's card) and the memo stays with the note being written.</li><li>The voice screen shows "Stopped listening"; the log records `ended: "stopped"`; the next conversation isn't told it was cut off.</li></ul>Not covered: changing the OpenAI test voice's mute (its engine also plays Doddy's voice), Siri's own listening, the phone agent. | Core + Linux tests (9 new); app wiring parse-checked; not compiled with Xcode; not run on a device (Mac checklist 4q, LS1–LS8) |

## Unfinished notes (2026-10-11, owner: "start OP-042"; numbered 362 (first 340), as above)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 362 | **Unfinished notes survive closing and crashes, as drafts, never notes** (OP-042, resume interrupted user draft; owner: "start OP-042"; `docs/implementation/UNFINISHED_NOTES_STATUS.md`).<ul><li>**Kept as typed:** the capture screen's words, type and shortcut flag are written to a protected file on this iPhone (Application Support/Drafts, complete file protection), 0.6 s after typing and at once on going to the background or closing the screen. Each write is read back. Each capture screen has its own draft id.</li><li>**Draft, not a note:** not searched, sent to the voice agent, exported or backed up. Only Save makes a note, and then the draft is removed; Discard and Delete all remove it too.</li><li>**Coming back:** Home shows "Unfinished note" with Continue and Discard. An empty capture screen opens with the newest one ("It isn't saved until you tap Save"), with Start a new note instead. Shortcut text is never mixed with a draft. Cancel also offers Keep it for later, only when the copy is confirmed.</li><li>**Limits:** 5 at most, 30 days untouched. Photos and recordings aren't kept with a draft; the resumed screen says to add them again.</li><li>**Failures:** a write that can't be confirmed is shown on the screen. A file Doddy can't read is set aside, not overwritten.</li></ul>Not covered: the voice screen's dropped-conversation draft (kept on screen only, ADR-360), other screens' edits, sync to other devices. | Core + Linux tests (7 new); the store run against real files by `scripts/check_unfinished_note_store.sh` (8 checks, in `ci.sh`); app screens parse-checked; not compiled with Xcode; not run on a device (Mac checklist 4r, UN1–UN6) |

## Listen until I finish (2026-10-11, owner: "do"; OP-019)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 364 | **"Listen until I finish": the user ends the turn, and only the words are sent** (OP-019, voice hold-to-talk; owner: "do"; `docs/implementation/LONG_TURN_STATUS.md`).<ul><li>A button on the voice screen mutes the conversation's microphone (so the voice service hears nothing and can't end the turn on a pause) and records the turn on this iPhone, up to 3 minutes.</li><li>On Done it is written out on the device (the voice-memo transcriber, ADR-230; never a language other than the conversation's), tidied, cut at 4,000 characters, and sent as one message (`sendMessage`); then the recording is deleted and the microphone goes back to how it was.</li><li>Anything that can't be written out or sent becomes a draft on the screen to retry, type or drop; the recording is kept only to write out again after a failed write-out.</li><li>Every ending (End, Stop listening, a drop, a failure) deletes it. The listening indicator says "Listening until you finish".</li></ul>Not covered: starting it by voice (needs an agent tool and a live agent update). | Core + Linux tests (5 new); app wiring parse-checked; not compiled with Xcode; not run on a device (Mac checklist 4s, LT1–LT6) |

## Watch, iPad and Mac (2026-10-11, owner: "forget iphone and continue")
The owner asked to run `prompts/APPLE_PLATFORMS_WATCH_IPAD_MAC.md` in a Linux cloud session. Its Phase 1 (iPhone build) was BLOCKED: no Xcode, and the hosted CI jobs end within seconds without a runner (no logs). The owner then said to skip the iPhone gate and continue. Open pull requests hold ADR-319–328, 360–362 and 401–403, so this round starts at ADR-430. The default branch then used ADR-440 and ADR-441 for the learning loop, so this round's 440–446 were renumbered 450–456 when it was merged in. **Nothing in this round has been compiled with Xcode or run on a simulator or device.** DoddyCore tests (863) pass on Linux.

Memories and Settings start at their top after a switch on iPad (one screen at a time in the detail column); Home keeps its place.</li></ul> `docs/implementation/IPAD_STATUS.md`. | Applied — core logic PASS (Linux); SwiftUI NOT RUN |
