The Blueprint kept 18 keys in the environment group `doddy-messaging-secrets`, all `sync: false`. Render's Blueprint spec says "you can't define an environment variable with `sync: false` in an environment group" and ignores them, so Render would never have asked for them. For service variables it says it asks for `sync: false` values "only during the initial Blueprint creation" and "ignores" them on later updates, so values typed once are kept. So the group is removed and the same 18 keys sit on `doddy-messaging` and `doddy-messaging-worker`. Render asks for them twice when the Blueprint is created; afterwards `tools/secrets/sync.mjs` (ADR-493) can fill both from 1Password. Non-secret settings on the services are unchanged. The Blueprint has never been applied (the running Render services were made by hand), so nothing live changes. | Applied 2026-10-18: `render.yaml` parses (PyYAML), both services list the same 18 `sync: false` keys, guard and `sync.mjs check` PASS. Not applied on Render; the owner's choice when to |
| 496 | **One voice conversation at a time across the app** (owner: "yes build it", 18 Oct; investigation `docs/implementation/VOICE_SESSION_STARTS.md`). The voice screen and "Hey Doddy" each make their own `VoiceSessionController`, and `guard !isLive` and the attempt numbers (review H1) only work inside one controller, so two could start at once. (1) **DoddyCore `VoiceConversationGate`:** a start goes ahead only if no *other* conversation is starting, connecting or talking. It remembers who asked last (weakly) and asks whether that one is still busy, instead of waiting to be told it ended, so a failed, never-started or freed conversation can't leave voice locked. The same conversation asking again ("Talk again") always goes ahead. (2) **`VoiceSessionController`:** `start` asks the gate first and does nothing (one log line) when another is running. A start counts as busy from the moment it's called (a counter, so a double tap can't clear it), not only once it's connecting. (3) **The voice screen** shows the conversation already running, such as the one "Hey Doddy" just started, instead of making a second. A shortcut's greeting, translator, check-in or note meant for a new conversation is dropped, so it can't start with a later one by surprise; the wake listener is told that conversation has been shown. (4) **"Hey Doddy"** is ignored while a conversation is running, and isn't counted towards the hourly limit. Known edge: a voice screen dismissed and shown again in the same moment can now end with no conversation (the old screen's stop ends the one the new screen picked up), where before it could end with two; tapping Talk again recovers. This doesn't address the silent 30-second "Hey Doddy" starts from the background, which still need a phone log. | Applied 2026-10-18 on Linux: DoddyCore `VoiceConversationGateTests` (6) PASS; the wiring pattern compiled and ran in Swift 6 mode on a stand-in (two starts at once: one goes, one refused, while the first is still before connecting); the three app files parse with `swiftc` 6.1. **iOS build NOT RUN** (ElevenLabs/UIKit/SwiftUI need Xcode; hosted CI isn't starting jobs) and never tried on a device |
| 497 | **Hosted CI: the macOS build runs only after the Linux checks pass, and on a pull request only when the change can reach the app** (owner: "yes do it", 18 Oct, after the Actions investigation). Why: the repository is private, so Actions time is paid for. *(Corrected 19 Oct, ADR-543: the repository is public, and the owner keeps it public. Hosted runners for public repositories are free, so the cost reasoning here no longer holds; the gating still saves time.)* From 12 Oct 19:36 UTC to 14 Oct 19:51 UTC the runs used 3,975 billed macOS minutes and 566 Linux minutes (about $246 + $3 at $0.062 and $0.006 a minute). Then jobs stopped starting again (16 Oct onwards, no runner assigned, as on 10–11 Oct): an account billing limit, which only the owner can see and change. A successful macOS job takes a median 52 minutes, of which the 29 UI tests take 34 (one test, `MemoryJourneyTests.testApprovedJourneyCorrectionRetainsOriginalAfterRestart`, takes 8.4). What changed in `.github/workflows/ios.yml`: (1) a new `changes` job (Ubuntu, a depth-2 checkout of the pull request's test merge commit) lists the files the pull request changes and runs `scripts/ci_needs_mac.sh`; (2) the macOS `ci` job `needs: [changes, core]` and runs only when `changes` says so. The rule: the Mac runs unless **every** changed file is in a fixed list of areas no Xcode target reads (docs, top-level Markdown, agent notes, servers, site, secrets, design sources, translation sources, archives, deploy files, and the voice-agent files other than the three the app bundles). Anything else, a new folder included, runs it, as does a run by hand, a file list that can't be computed, or an empty one. So the list can only be wrong by costing money. `scripts/test_ci_needs_mac.sh` (31 cases, in `scripts/ci.sh`) checks it. Measured on the 102 runs (108 macOS jobs) of 12–14 Oct, this would have saved about 240 of 3,975 minutes (about $15): 158 on pull requests with no app change, 64 on runs whose Linux checks failed, and about 21 on pushes replaced within the Linux job's 4–5 minutes. Most pushes did change the app. Costs: the Mac build starts about 4–5 minutes later, and a Linux-only failure (the app logic type-check) now holds the Mac build back too. Not done, owner's call: run the UI tests (two thirds of the Mac time) only on request, or speed up the slowest UI tests | Applied 2026-10-18. Linux: `scripts/test_ci_needs_mac.sh` 31/31 PASS, and four planted mistakes in the rule each make it fail; the workflow's own step, taken from the YAML, run on depth-2 clones of test merge commits: PR #84 → Mac runs, a docs-only change → skipped, a non-merge checkout → Mac runs; `actionlint` 1.7.7 reports no problems. **Not run on GitHub** (Actions isn't starting jobs on this account) |
| 498 | **Vercel: only the production branch deploys; working `claude/…` branches make no deployments** (owner: "yes add it", 18 Oct). Why: the team is on the free plan, which allows 100 deployments a day, and each push to any branch makes three (site, gateway, voice-token), including ones each project's `ignoreCommand` then cancels. On 18 Oct the team had 100 deployments between 09:34 and 12:22 UTC across 12 branches, 86 of them cancelled and 2 built; from then on Vercel refused new ones ("Deployment rate limited", "Account is blocked" on the pull request checks), as on 13 Oct (`site/README.md`). What: `site/vercel.json`, `gateway/vercel.json` and `server/voice-token/vercel.json` get `"git": {"deploymentEnabled": {"claude/!(loving-dirac-uuxoux)": false}}`. One pattern rather than "all `claude/**` off, production on", because Vercel's docs don't say which wins when two patterns match. If Vercel didn't support the `!(…)` form, the pattern would match no branch and deployments would carry on as before; it can't stop production. Vercel reads the file from the commit being pushed, so the rule covers a branch once that branch contains it: after this is merged, new branches from the default branch and branches that merge it. `ignoreCommand` stays for the production branch. Costs: pull requests get no Vercel preview links or checks; branches outside `claude/` (`codex/…`, `vercel/…`, `work`) still deploy; if the production branch is ever renamed to another `claude/…` name, all three files must change first | Applied 2026-10-18. Pattern checked with minimatch 3.1.2, 5.1.6, 9.0.5 and 10.0.1 (the matcher Vercel's docs name): production deploys, every other `claude/` branch is off, `claude/loving-dirac-uuxoux-2` is off, names outside `claude/` and deeper names still deploy; of the repository's 131 branches, 126 are off. All three files validate against Vercel's published `vercel.json` schema. **Not yet seen working on Vercel:** after the merge, a push to a working branch should create no deployment, and a push to the production branch should still deploy |
| 499 | **UI tests: `MemoryJourneyTests` stops scrolling once a button is tappable and a scroll step no longer moves it** (owner: "do it", 18 Oct, after ADR-497 named the slow UI tests as the bigger saving). Why: on CI (run of 14 Oct, the last that ran) the three `MemoryJourneyTests` took 501 s, 158 s and 150 s of the 34 minutes of UI tests. The log shows 205 scroll drags, about 3 s each, and five of the test's `scrollTo` calls (before Save, Done and "Original capture" in the first test, before Save in each of the others) ran all 40 of their steps: the button was already tappable but couldn't be moved clear of the tab bar's margin (a pinned button, or the end of the page), so the loop never ended early, and the tap afterwards worked. What: `scrollTo` remembers where the element was before each step; if it is tappable and the step moved it less than a point, it stops. It only stops early when the element is tappable, so the test's next tap behaves exactly as before; a button that isn't tappable keeps the old 40 steps. Expected: about 195 fewer drags, roughly 10 minutes off each macOS run (about $0.60), with no test removed or weakened. The other UI tests' scrolling (Settings via `reveal`) never hit its cap and is unchanged | Applied 2026-10-18. The edited function's own text, compiled in Swift 6 mode against stand-ins of the XCUITest members it uses, in a simulated page: a pinned button under the bar's margin stops after 1 step (the old text: 40), a button at the end of a page after 2 (old: 40), a far-down button, a lazily created row and a button already in view take the same steps as before, and a never-tappable button keeps 40; the file parses with `swiftc` 6.1. **UI tests NOT RUN** (no Xcode here; hosted CI isn't starting jobs), so the time saved is an estimate from the 14 Oct log |

| 522 | **Voice needs a DODDY voice account made with Sign in with Apple; the tester access code and the public agent are retired** (the owner's commit `754e5d6`, "Add first-launch profile flow and secure voice access", 16 October; it had no ADR, recorded 18 October for checklist A6). <ul><li>**What the user sees:** on first voice use, Sign in with Apple, then one consent switch per provider ("I agree to send voice data to …", kept in UserDefaults). Settings gains Sign out of voice, Ask again before sharing voice, and Delete voice account. Notes, lists and everything without voice need no account.</li><li>**How it works:** the server checks Apple's identity token (Apple's keys, issuer, audience = `APPLE_BUNDLE_ID`, expiry, hashed nonce). Doddy allowed 50 note-reminder slots plus 20 birthdays plus its own routines and timers, so a full setup could lose some quietly.</li><li>**The rule** (`NotificationBudget` in DoddyCore): 14 slots are always left for what Doddy schedules itself (morning check-in up to 5, evening wrap-up, weekly look-back, tidy-up, plus timers, focus, leave-by, snoozes, event nudges). Note reminders and birthdays share the other 50: each may hold 64 − the other one − max(14, everything else). The old caps (50 slots, 20 birthdays) still apply on top.</li><li>**What the user sees:** setting a note reminder past the room says the limit as before, now with the lower number (for example "up to 24" with 20 birthdays on). Birthdays keep the soonest ones that fit; the screen says how many were set, as before.</li><li>**Not covered:** Doddy's own schedulers (morning check-in, evening, weekly, tidy-up, timers, focus, leave-by, nudges) don't consult the budget yet. If together they go past 14 while note reminders and birthdays are full, iOS can still drop the latest ones; the budget only stops user-set kinds from adding more. The birthday screen doesn't say why fewer were set than picked.</li></ul> | **Applied, tested on Linux:** `NotificationBudgetTests` (7) and `NoteReminderCoordinatorTests.testTheLimitLeavesRoomForBirthdaysAndDoddysOwnNotifications` pass. `BirthdayViews.swift` passes the pending list in: NOT COMPILED (SwiftUI). Not tried on a device |
| 524 | **Deleting the voice account also revokes Sign in with Apple, once the owner adds a Sign in with Apple key** (owner: "Yes", 18 October, to fixing go-live checklist C8; App Store guideline 5.1.1(v)). <ul><li>**Sign-in:** the app also sends Apple's one-time `authorizationCode`. With `APPLE_TEAM_ID`, `APPLE_SIGN_IN_KEY_ID` and `APPLE_SIGN_IN_PRIVATE_KEY` set, the server swaps it at Apple's `/auth/token` (ES256 client secret, 5 minutes) and keeps Apple's refresh token only if Apple's `id_token` names the same person just verified. It is stored sealed (AES-256-GCM, key derived from `DODDY_SESSION_SIGNING_KEY`), never logged, and expires with the session (30 days, extended on refresh). Any failure leaves sign-in working.</li><li>**Deletion:** the server posts the token to Apple's `/auth/revoke`, then deletes everything, the sealed token included. Apple failing or taking over 5 seconds never keeps the account. The reply adds `appleRevoked`; the log line says `revoked`, `failed`, `no_token` or `not_configured`.</li><li>**Without the key:** nothing changes and nothing is sent to Apple's token endpoints. Accounts made before the key is set can't be revoked until they sign in again.</li><li>**Not changed:** the delete dialog still tells people to also remove DODDY in their Apple ID settings. That stays true until the key is set and checked, and changing it would mean new strings in every language.</li></ul> `server/voice-token/README.md`, `secrets/manifest.json` (the key is a 1Password secret; team ID and key ID aren't secrets) and the privacy draft §5.2 are updated. | **Applied, server tested on Linux** (`server/voice-token`: 25 tests pass, Apple's endpoints faked). The app's `VoiceTokenClient` and `finishAppleSignIn` type-check on Linux with stand-ins; `VoiceAccessGate.swift` (reads the code from Apple's credential) is NOT COMPILED. Never run against Apple: no Sign in with Apple key is set on the server |

## Doddy calls on your behalf (2026-10-18)

Owner brief: Doddy makes short outbound calls in Polish for the user, asks first, and brings the answer back as a card. Numbered from 500 to stay clear of numbers open branches are taking. Architecture, docs checked and open legal questions: `docs/decisions/calls.md`. Status and live-test steps: `docs/implementation/CALL_CASES_STATUS.md`. Nothing here has been heard on a real call yet.

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 500 | **A call case: Doddy places a call only after the user approves a card built by the server, and exactly that card is sent.** Our server places the Twilio call and uses ElevenLabs only as the voice (register-call), not ElevenLabs' native outbound call, so limits, callbacks and the kill switch stay ours. The brief is compiled once into the full provider payload (Twilio `To`/`From`/`TimeLimit`/`Timeout`/`Record=false` plus the agent's variables), encrypted, and approved by its HMAC digest; a composite foreign key ties every attempt to that digest, and a dial claim is committed before Twilio is called (timeout = `unknown`, never redialled). Editing makes a new case. **Supersedes ADR-065 for approved call cases only**: links still never place a call, and the voice conversation can't start one. | Server tests (real Postgres, fake Twilio and ElevenLabs) cover payload equality with what Twilio and register-call receive, digest binding, concurrency (one `Calls.create` for parallel approvals/dials) and unknown-never-redialled. Not run: a live call. |
| 501 | **Only ticked facts leave the iPhone; the number says where it came from.** The brief editor keeps unticked facts on the iPhone. A PESEL (checksum) or an address is sent only as a fact ticked and marked as such; card numbers and Polish IBANs are refused; a question that books, orders, pays or confirms is refused. Numbers come from Apple's contact picker (no Contacts permission; only the tapped number and that contact's name), a note or typing, and the card shows which. | Server `checkBriefContent` and DoddyCore `CallBriefDraft.problems` tested; the editor and contact picker are not compiled here. |
| 502 | **The first thing said is fixed and word for word:** "Dzień dobry, mówi Doddy, asystent AI, dzwonię w imieniu <imię w dopełniaczu>." + optional notice + the question, as the agent's `first_message` with interruptions off. The recording notice is `CALL_RECORDING_NOTICE` = `off` (default until the owner confirms the legal basis), `nagrywana` or `zapisywana`. Audio is never recorded (Twilio `Record=false`, ElevenLabs `record_voice=false`); a text transcript always is. Open legal questions are in calls.md. | Opening line tests; agent spec check in CI (`voice_agent_spec.py --agent task-call`) and drift check against the live agent. Wording not reviewed by a lawyer. |
| 503 | **Hard limits live in the backend:** Polish mobile, geographic and 800 numbers only (emergency, short, premium, shared-cost, foreign, the user's and Doddy's own numbers refused); the whole call must fit inside 8:00–20:00 Europe/Warsaw; per-user, per-destination and global daily caps from a content-free dial log; one live call per account; `TASK_CALLS_KILL_SWITCH`; dial and answer refused while the live ElevenLabs agent differs from its spec. Env values can only tighten code ceilings. | Table tests of every 2-digit prefix, DST, each limit, kill switch and drift at create, approve, dial and answer. |
| 504 | **During the call:** live transcript from Twilio Real-Time Transcription (ElevenLabs' live monitoring is Enterprise-only), polled by the app every second; the Live Activity shows phase and call time only, moved by APNs `liveactivity` pushes, with Take over and End buttons that need the iPhone unlocked. "Przejmij" rings the user's verified phone; pressing 1 moves the callee into a conference with them; Doddy carries on until then. | Takeover state machine, conference watchdog and push payload tested on the server. Live Activity, widget and intents not compiled here. |
| 505 | **The answer card quotes the transcript, never a model's words.** The quote is always a span of the callee's own turn; Claude Haiku 5.5, if configured, may only point at the turn and span and write the summary and next step. Confidence is computed in code; Doddy's own lines are audited for commitment words and numbers not on the card. Failed calls get an honest card; no answer, busy, voicemail, phone menu, wrong number and technical failures can be retried at a set time, as a fresh approval of the same card (at most two retries). | Result builder tests (quote always a span, model quote rejected when not verbatim, audit). Retry after voicemail tested. App screens not compiled here. |
| 506 | **The transcript ends up only on the iPhone; every server deletion leaves a receipt.** The app saves the answer card (read back from SQLite, migration `v14_call_cases`) before acknowledging it; the acknowledgement deletes the server's result, live lines and (unless a retry is possible) the payload, each with a `server_data_deletions` receipt in the same transaction. The ElevenLabs conversation is deleted after processing and counts as deleted only when a read returns 404; an orphan sweep and a 7-day expiry cover the rest. "Logged per Prompt 2" was read as this content-free receipt log, since no Prompt 2 was found in the repo. **Every dialled attempt has cost items** (Twilio price, transcription, ElevenLabs credits, model tokens), written with the dial claim; unknown is never shown as zero. | Server deletion, receipt and cost tests; app save-then-acknowledge order tested with fakes (`scripts/test_call_cases_model.sh`, also in Xcode as `DoddyTests/CallCasesModelTests.swift`). |
| 507 | **Rehearsal before the call** (owner backlog #20/#116): Doddy plays the person who answers, in Polish, on the app's OpenAI Realtime voice with an empty tool list; nobody is called and nothing is saved. Afterwards "Doddy dzwoni" (back to the card) or "Dzwonię sam" (the script on screen and a `tel:` link). Its cost is not tracked on the case in v1. | Prompt and script tested in DoddyCore; the session and screen are not compiled or heard here. |

## BLIK transfers (2026-10-18)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 510 | **Doddy prepares BLIK phone transfers; the user sends them in their bank app** (owner: "Can you also connect BLIK payments to Dodi?", then chose "Prepare it, I send it" over taking BLIK payments for Doddy Pro or Doddy sending money itself). BLIK has no public interface for an app to send money: a phone transfer ("przelew na telefon") is made only in the user's own bank app, and developer access exists only for merchants taking BLIK at checkout through payment providers. So payments stay a non-goal (PRD) and this is a hand-off, like `draft_message`. (1) New phone tool `draft_blik_transfer` (`to`, `amount` in złoty, optional `title` and `currency`) behind its own switch, Settings › Connections › Money › BLIK transfers, off by default; no iOS permission. (2) Typed code decides, not the prompt (`BlikTransfer.swift`): the amount is kept in grosze and never rounded; more than two decimals, zero or less, another currency or more than 10,000 zł (Doddy's own ceiling, not a bank's; the owner can change `BlikTransferLimits.maxGrosze`) is refused and the agent asks again. (3) Contacts is used only when iOS allows it, to add the person's numbers; several contacts with the name are never guessed between (the agent asks); no match shows the name only. Numbers and the title can be copied (this iPhone only, gone after 10 minutes); the amount is shown, not copied. (4) The card's confirm button is "I'll send it myself": nothing runs, the details stay on screen until dismissed, and the agent hears `shown:` with "Nothing was sent … never say it was sent, paid or done". What Doddy did gets a new result, "Shown for you to send", and keeps only "to <name>" for 30 days, not the amount. (5) Never in a plan, never while driving (CarProfile), stopped by Pause all and by translator mode. (6) `changesSomething` still named the translator card that ADR-458's follow-up removed (f40861c), which stopped the app compiling; this branch and ASK-01 (ADR-483) both removed it. Numbered 510 because 483 to 502 were taken on other branches (ASK-01 took 483). (7) Under ASK-01 (ADR-483) the tool is `ask: tap` and `AskFirst` counts it as an own-card tool: the BLIK card is the ask, and its tap still sends nothing. | Built: DoddyCore tests on Linux (15 new; 1,128 pass after merging ASK-01); voice spec check PASS (82 tools); strings catalogs regenerated with Polish lines. App code (card, Connections) not compiled: no Mac here. Live ElevenLabs agent synced 2026-10-18 (tool `tool_5701m57c1717fmebcs14kbrjqmdq`, version `agtvrsn_0201m57cm3vnfgsrv3fw49mqb9mk`, rollback `agtvrsn_3501m5219zqke9tv2thaxz3m729t`; read back byte for byte); OpenAI Realtime gets the tool with the next app build. Not seen on a device. |

## Motion v2: one persistent Doddy (2026-10-19; drafted as 550 and 551, renumbered 554 and 555 when #108 merged first with 546 to 551; 552 keeps its number, 553 is #113's)

At you the picture is exactly as the pack drew it. (5) **Known limits:** the paper is not perfectly flat, so the cover can show as a faint patch at high magnification (proof sheets at 13x contrast show a faint smooth patch, no ring; 1 to 3 levels of 255); the eyes jump between eight looks rather than gliding; the evening blink plays on the wave picture. (6) **Also fixed, found by this branch's hosted run (39379870387):** `MotionEngine.carried(to:)` now restarts his idle, as a landing does. ## Backend database on Postgres (2026-10-19)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 525 | **The Connected Apps backend keeps its data in Postgres, not in a `node:sqlite` file** (owner: "lets do database that is commercially viable", then "da sie cos bez kosztow takich narazie do testowania ale z innnymi": free for testing, other providers later). Render's free plan has no disk, so the SQLite file was lost on every sleep, restart and deploy: connections, receipts, approvals and usage counters with it. <ul><li>**Production:** any hosted Postgres through `DATABASE_URL` (`pg` 8.23.1). For testing that is the free Supabase project `doddy-gateway`, through its session pooler (port 5432). Moving to Render Postgres, Neon or another Postgres later is a new `DATABASE_URL`; no code change.</li><li>**Tests, mock mode and local development:** PGlite 0.5.8, the same Postgres engine in the process, in memory or in a data directory (`DODDY_DB_PATH`). One SQL dialect everywhere.</li><li>**Separate schema** `doddy_backend`, so a shared database keeps these tables apart. Its own login role, without access to anything else; the `anon` and `authenticated` API roles get nothing on it.</li><li>**Same guarantees as before:** every multi-step change runs in one SERIALIZABLE transaction, retried on a serialization conflict (at most 5 tries); approvals stay single-use; usage is reserved before a provider call. Timestamps stay millisecond integers.</li><li>**TLS** is always verified outside loopback; `DATABASE_CA_CERT` supplies a private CA (Supabase's root). Search path and a 10-second statement timeout are set with a plain `SET` on each connection, since poolers can refuse startup parameters.</li><li>**`DODDY_ENV=production` requires `DATABASE_URL`**, so a production server can't silently fall back to a store it loses.</li><li>**Not moved:** the store's tables and what they hold (no email, event or note content). The data in the old SQLite file is not migrated: it was already lost at each restart.</li></ul> | Built: 242 backend tests pass on PGlite (Node 22). **Against a hosted Postgres: NOT RUN yet** (this container can't reach the database port); to be checked on Render |

## Doddy accounts: Sign in with Apple or Google (2026-10-19)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 526 | **A Doddy account is a Supabase Auth user, made with Sign in with Apple or Google** (owner: "do sign in with google and apple", then chose "Supabase Auth" over extending the voice server's own Apple sign-in). It is what lets every user connect and use their own Gmail and other accounts instead of the owner's minted token. <ul><li>**How it works:** the app gets an identity token from Apple or Google and exchanges it with Supabase Auth (project `doddy-gateway`) for a session: an access token (1 hour) and a refresh token that rotates.</li><li>**The Connected Apps server checks the access token itself:** <ul><li>the ES256 or RS256 signature, against the project's published keys (no shared secret; HS256 refused);</li><li>the issuer, the audience `authenticated`, the role and the expiry;</li><li>anonymous Supabase users are refused, and so is any sign-in method other than Apple or Google (`app_metadata.provider`), so leaving email sign-up on in Supabase can't open this server.</li></ul> The user ID is the Supabase user's UUID.</li><li>**Keys:** cached 10 minutes. An unknown key ID fetches them again at most every 30 seconds, so junk tokens can't flood Supabase. While Supabase is unreachable the cached keys keep working; with none yet, the reply is 502 "couldn't check your sign-in, try again", never "signed out".</li><li>**Modes:** `DODDY_AUTH_MODE=supabase` with `SUPABASE_URL`. In development the owner's minted token is still accepted next to it while `DODDY_SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` is set. Production takes Supabase sign-ins only, and production live mode now starts with this mode instead of being refused outright.</li><li>**Cost:** Supabase's free plan covers 50,000 monthly active users. It pauses the project after a week without use; Pro, $25 a month, removes the pause.</li><li>**Not built yet:** <ul><li>the app's sign-in screen (Apple button, Google), keeping the session in the Keychain, refreshing it, and sending it instead of the pasted token;</li><li>deleting the account (App Store 5.1.1(v)): a server route that removes the Composio connections, this server's rows and the Supabase user (needs the project's secret key on the server), and revokes Sign in with Apple as ADR-524 does;</li><li>the voice server accepting the same session instead of its own Apple sign-in, which would replace that part of ADR-522.</li></ul></li><li>**Owner steps:** in Supabase, Authentication → Providers: Apple on (Client IDs: the bundle ID), Google on (Client IDs: an iOS OAuth client from Google Cloud with the bundle ID). Apple needs the paid developer team (checklist A5).</li></ul> `docs/integrations/DEPLOY_RENDER.md` "Sign-in with Apple or Google". | Server part built: 11 new tests, the verifier loads the real project's key. **App: NOT BUILT.** Not switched on on Render yet. |

| 567 | **Presentations: Doddy makes a PowerPoint file that Keynote opens** (owner, 20 Oct: "You should be able to do presentations"). The app writes it to its temporary folder, reads it back, and opens iOS's share sheet (Keynote, Files, AirDrop and the rest are iOS's).</li><li>**What the agent hears:** `made:` with the slide count; never that it was opened, saved or sent, which Doddy can't see.</li><li>**Connection `presentations`** in Settings, Connections (no iOS permission), off like every other.</li><li>**Evidence:** DoddyCore tests (parse, limits, file name, escaping, CRC, the package read back part by part, every part well-formed XML); a sample deck opened with python-pptx 1.0.2 (4 slides, all text read back) and rendered by LibreOffice to PNG. ## Owner report, 21 October 2026 (Warsaw): Polish first, a translator that translates, lookups that just answer

| ADR | Decision | Status |

| 573 | **Polish is the default language for Doddy's screens and his voice** (owner, 21 Oct: "you changed the language to be English default, and it should be Polish"). Supersedes ADR-459's English default. <ul><li>**Screens:** `AppLanguage.default` is `.polish`. Builds before this wrote `["en"]` to the app's `AppleLanguages` on first launch, which reads like a choice, so once (`polishDefaultApplied` false) that exact English is replaced by Polish when nothing was chosen in Doddy; English picked in Doddy, or set again in iOS Settings › Doddy afterwards, stays. iOS has already chosen the language of the launch that writes it, so Polish shows from the next launch.</li><li>**Voice:** `ConversationLanguage.default` is `.polish` (was `auto`, which starts in English). A stored "Automatic" stays automatic; Doddy still switches when the user speaks another language. Settings › Voice › Starts in shows Polish when nothing is stored. The opening line is the Polish one, and translator mode's "You speak" defaults to Polish.</li><li>**Live agent:** the owner set the agent's own default language to Polish and added an English preset in the dashboard on 20 Oct at 23:25 and 23:34 UTC (`agtvrsn_6501m5dt5bgtecetpe5eemvvp78d` "Set default language to Polish", then `agtvrsn_5201m5dtmpvne48vt1hxc6h53hh0`); this change didn't touch them.</li></ul> | Built. DoddyCore tests on Linux (`AppLanguageTests`, `NicknameTests`). The launch write and the Settings picker are app code: not compiled here, not seen on a device. |

## Owner, 22 October 2026: the Action Button opens Doddy

| ADR | Decision | Status |

| 593 | **The Action Button opens Doddy again; "Talk in the Island" is no longer a control** (owner, 22 Oct: "make it so when I press the Action Button the Doddy app opens, not the Dynamic Island alone with Doddy"). Reverses the Action Button part of ADR-577. <ul><li>`TalkInIslandControl` keeps its kind (`com.bienos.doddy2.talk-island`), because iOS remembers a placed control by its kind, but now runs `TalkToDoddyIntent` (`openAppWhenRun = true`) and is named "Talk to Doddy" with the same description and icon as `TalkToDoddyControl`. An Action Button, Control Center or Lock Screen already set to it opens Doddy and starts the conversation after the update, with nothing to change in Settings; Settings › Back Tap and Siri › "Only open Doddy" applies to it as to every Talk to Doddy start.</li><li>The control gallery now shows two "Talk to Doddy" controls that do the same thing. Removing the old one would leave an Action Button set to it doing nothing, so both stay.</li><li>`TalkInIslandIntent`, `TalkInIsland.start()` and the island that opens up when Doddy speaks (`IslandExpansion`) are unchanged; the intent stays in the Shortcuts app as "Talk in the Island", run by no control.</li><li>"Ways to open Doddy" › Action Button no longer offers "Talk in the Island"; its steps already said "Pick Talk to Doddy".</li><li>Numbered 593 because 579 to 592 are taken on open branches (`claude/gifted-pascal-3e117c`, `claude/exciting-carson-08x2pk`, `claude/confident-shannon-wrux16`, `claude/release-0-1`).</li></ul> | **Superseded on 24 Oct 2026 by ADR-632: the owner wants the Action Button in the Dynamic Island again.** Not compiled: this machine has no Swift toolchain or Xcode, so not even `swiftc -parse` ran. Linux checks run: string catalogs up to date, forbidden-capability guard and platform specs PASS; the island art check did not run (it needs `swiftc`). Not seen on a device: whether iOS swaps the control's action without re-picking it in Settings › Action Button is iOS's to do on the update; if the button still starts the island, pick Talk to Doddy there once. |

The rule is `WelcomeRule` in DoddyCore.</li><li>**Sign-in is UI only:** `SignInProviding` and `SignInOption` (face ID, Google, email) in DoddyCore; the only provider, `UnconnectedSignIn`, returns `.notConnectedYet`, and a test keeps `.signedIn` unreachable. The Face ID scan animation is only the pressed state and checks nothing. No backend, SDK, Info.plist or entitlement change.</li><li>**Custom sheet, not a system sheet:** a system `.sheet` would be presented above the Face ID lock's cover (RootView draws the lock as an overlay) and the lock must stay above everything. Two heights, a drag that follows the finger, spring on release; Reduce Transparency makes it opaque.</li><li>**Face ID boundary kept:** the button's name comes from `AppLock.biometry` (read only, no prompt); `LocalAuthentication` is still imported only in `AppLock.swift` (`check_forbidden_capabilities.sh` passes).</li><li>**Placeholders and owner decisions:** the Warsaw line and the pigeon are my own vector placeholders (`WelcomeScenery.swift`), to be replaced by the owner's pack scenery; the hand-written lines use a system stand-in (`Theme.hand`) because Caveat is not bundled (decision: bundle Caveat under the OFL?); Polish strings are hand-written for the screens, the other eight languages fall back to English until `tools/l10n_batch.py` runs; the Palace of Culture outline is simplified and unverified.</li><li>**App Review risk, not fixed here:** Google sign-in needs an equivalent privacy-preserving option such as Sign in with Apple (guideline 4.8); GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST C16.</li></ul> | Built on `claude/gifted-einstein-61p6hq`. DoddyCore: `WelcomeFlowTests`, `SignInOptionTests`, `WelcomePathDataTests` written in an environment with no Swift toolchain, then run by PR #132's hosted CI (run 39872214301, head `5e2e654`): the `core` and macOS jobs are green; the macOS job built the app, ran the unit tests and 60 UI tests (1 skipped, 0 failures), including the 6 `WelcomeUITests`, and `scripts/ci.sh` ended RESULT: PASS. Also run locally: `check_forbidden_capabilities.sh` PASS, `build_strings_catalog.py --check` PASS, `l10n_batch.py status pl` 0 missing. Never seen on a device. `docs/implementation/WELCOME_STATUS.md`. |
| 599 | **Sign in with Apple joins the welcome's sheet, and it is real: it is the Doddy voice account's sign-in** (owner, 23 Oct 2026: "Sign in with Apple", after C16 said App Review needs it next to Google). <ul><li>**One button, one place:** the voice screen's Apple button is now `AppleAccountSignInButton` in `VoiceAccessGate.swift` (the file the capability guard already allows for AuthenticationServices), used by the voice gate and by the welcome's `SignInSheet`. The request, nonce and answer handling are the voice gate's, moved, not changed (ADR-484, ADR-494, ADR-524). `check_forbidden_capabilities.sh` is unchanged and passes.</li><li>**What it does in the welcome:** Apple's system button, titled "Continue with Apple", first in the list and the same height as the others (Apple's rule), a capsule to match them, black on light and white on dark. A finished sign-in (`VoiceAuthSession.isSignedIn`) ends the welcome; Apple's failures show in the voice account's words; cancelling is silent. It creates the voice account for the voice the person has chosen (ElevenLabs by default). Nothing else about Doddy's identity changes: the device session of ADR-563 still serves web search and online accounts.</li><li>**Still not connected:** Face ID, Google and email (ADR-598). The sheet now reads "Apple, Face ID, Google or email", and is 446 pt tall open.</li><li>**What it needs to work:** the Sign in with Apple capability on the App ID (a paid team, GO_LIVE_CHECKLIST A5) and the voice token server answering (A4). On a build for a free team (`make project-iphone` drops the entitlements) Apple's sheet fails and the sheet says so; "Try Doddy without an account" still works. At the time of writing the Vercel account that hosts the token server shows "Account is blocked" on PR #132's checks.</li></ul> | Built on `claude/gifted-einstein-61p6hq`. Checks run here: capability guard PASS, string catalogs PASS, Polish status 0 missing. The UI test `testSignInOptionsSaySoAndOnlyTheNoAccountButtonMovesOn` now also checks that Apple's button exists and is as tall as Google's; the button is never tapped in tests (it opens Apple's sheet). **A real Apple sign-in has not been done on a device.** |


## Doddy Web, first slice (2026-10-22)

The owner's brief of 22 Oct ("DODDY — Master implementation prompt": "Build DODDY Web as a natural extension of the existing mobile application while establishing shared identity, memory, conversations, documents, tasks…", "Do not create an entirely separate AI assistant for the browser", "Respect any explicit milestone approval gates"). Phase 0 (audit) and Phase 1 (web foundation) only; everything after waits on the decisions in `docs/architecture/WEB_AND_SHARED_PLATFORM.md` section F. Built on `claude/exciting-carson-08x2pk`. These were first written as ADR-579 to 581; other branches of 22 Oct used 579 to 584, so they were renumbered 585 to 587 when the branches were merged (owner, 22 Oct: "i want all changes to be on app").

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 600 | **The iPhone vouches for a browser: Doddy Web signs in with a one-time code from the phone, and the browser gets a cookie session for the same server account** (owner's brief, 22 Oct: "A user can securely sign in to the web application and access a real authenticated DODDY workspace. No mocked account data"; "Existing mobile users must not lose access… Do not merge unrelated accounts automatically"; "Use secure server-side session handling for the browser"). The phone has no Apple or Google account on `backend/` yet (ADR-526's app side is unbuilt; ADR-563's device user is the account), so a browser can't sign in by itself without making a second, unrelated account. <ul><li>**Code:** `POST /v1/web/link-codes` with the phone's own session (Bearer only). Eight characters from 30 (no I, L, O, U, 0, 1), shown as `ABCD-EFGH`, valid 5 minutes, once, newest only; stored as SHA-256; at most 20 an hour per user. The reply carries the web origin; the phone shows it.</li><li>**Sign-in:** `POST /v1/web/session {code}` from the web app's own origin (`Origin` must match `DODDY_WEB_ORIGIN` and `x-doddy-web: 1`, also against login CSRF). Every try, right or wrong, is reserved atomically before the code is read: per client 10 per quarter hour (`DODDY_WEB_SIGNIN_TRIES_PER_CLIENT`; the last `X-Forwarded-For` hop behind `DODDY_TRUST_PROXY=1`; IPv6 by /64) and per server 2,000 an hour (high enough that one person can't easily lock everyone out; 30^8 codes keep guessing odds near 3 in a million an hour). Success: a random 256-bit token in `__Host-doddy_web` (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict, Path=/; `doddy_web` without Secure on http loopback), stored as SHA-256 with a coarse browser and system (never the User-Agent). 30 days, 14 idle, 10 per user (the oldest is signed out).</li><li>**What a browser session may do:** `GET/DELETE /v1/web/session`, `GET /v1/web/sessions`, `DELETE /v1/web/sessions/:id`, `GET /v1/web/documents`, `GET /v1/web/documents/:id/file`, and read `GET /v1/tools` and `GET /v1/tools/receipts`. Nothing else: no tool runs, switches, connections or new codes. Unsafe cookie requests need the same-origin check. The phone (Bearer) can list and sign out browsers too.</li><li>**Documents on the web:** the user's own rows of ADR-564's `documents` table, newest first, without bodies. A row still `working` 10 minutes after it started shows as `interrupted` (its job lived in a server process that stopped), never as still being made. Files download as attachments with `CSP: sandbox` and `nosniff`; another user's ID is 404. Still kept 7 days (durable storage is Phase 4).</li><li>**iPhone:** Settings › Doddy on the web: Get a sign-in code (code, address, countdown), Signed-in browsers with Sign out. `WebLinkClient` and `WebSignInModel` in DoddyCore (renews the device session once on `unauthenticated`); `DoddyApp/Features/Web/WebSignInView.swift`. What leaves this iPhone gains a "webSignIn" row: only a code request leaves the phone; the browser sees what the server already keeps, never notes.</li><li>**Off unless configured:** without `DODDY_WEB_ORIGIN` no web route or page exists.</li></ul> | Built on `claude/exciting-carson-08x2pk`. Backend `test/web.test.ts` 33 tests (after an independent security review: atomic try reservation, IPv6 /64, a higher server cap), full suite 307/307; web unit 25; Playwright 10 (desktop and phone width: sign-in with a phone code, download of a deck made through the phone's ASK-01 flow, phone signs the browser out, a used code refused, keyboard and skip link, headers); DoddyCore `WebLinkClientTests` 9, `WebSignInModelTests` 7, `DataFlowTests` +1, compiled with Swift 6.1.2 on Linux. **SwiftUI screen not compiled here** (hosted Mac CI is its first compile). **Not deployed, not run against the live server or on a device.** |
| 601 | **Doddy Web is a Vite + React + TypeScript app served by `backend/` on the same origin; no Next.js, no new host** (owner's brief: "Evaluate Next.js… but verify compatibility with the existing repository and deployment setup before committing to that choice", "Prefer extending the best existing backend", "Avoid adding … services … without a demonstrated capability gap"). <ul><li>**Why not Next.js on Vercel:** a second deployable and session store in front of `backend/`, server functions far from the Frankfurt backend, Vercel's free plan is non-commercial and the team already hit its 100 deploys a day (STATUS). A static SPA calling `backend/` cross-origin would need tokens in browser storage and CORS. Serving the built files from `backend/` keeps one origin, a first-party HttpOnly cookie, no CORS and no new monthly cost.</li><li>**Stack:** React 19.2, React Router 7, zod 3.25 (the backend's version) for every reply, Vite 7, TypeScript 5.9 (the backend's), Vitest 3 + Testing Library, Playwright 1.56 (the browser installed in the agent's environment). Newest majors (React 19.3, Router 8, Vite 8, TypeScript 7) were skipped as too new to be well known.</li><li>**Pages:** `backend/src/web/static.ts` serves `web/dist` (`DODDY_WEB_DIST`): files of known types only, no dotfiles or way out of the folder, hashed assets cached a year, everything else gets `index.html`; `/v1/…` never falls back. CSP `default-src 'self'` (no inline script or style), `frame-ancestors 'none'`, Permissions-Policy without camera, microphone or location, HSTS on https.</li><li>**Design:** colours and metrics generated from the app's `UploadedTokens.swift` (what `Theme` uses), light and dark, by `tools/gen_web_assets.py` (`--check` in CI); the app's wordmark SVG (ADR-562) and the pack's `WholeDoddy_crosslegged` pose (ADR-545's Home) copied byte for byte; system fonts, nothing bundled or fetched. Only working sections are in the menu.</li><li>**Language:** Polish by default (ADR-573), English; every string in `web/src/i18n/messages.ts`.</li><li>**Build:** `render.yaml` builds `web/` after `backend/`; the hand-made Render service needs the same build command (`docs/platforms/web.md`). `scripts/ci.sh` type-checks, tests and builds `web/`, and runs the end-to-end tests where a Playwright Chromium exists (else SKIPPED); `web/*` doesn't start the Mac job.</li></ul> | Built with ADR-600. Production bundle 97 KB of JavaScript gzipped, no inline scripts. The colours of the app's `UploadedTokens` (canvas `#F5EFE2`, orange cue `#EC803F`) differ from Brand Guidelines V2.2's paper `#FFF7E9` and orange `#FF6A00` that `site/tokens.css` uses; the web follows the app (CLAUDE.md: "the design is what the app shows"). Owner: say if the app and web should move to the guide's colours together. |

## Ask Doddy for everyone (2026-10-22)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 587 | **Typed Ask Doddy runs on Doddy's own server with Doddy's Anthropic key, and every iPhone can use it with no address or code** (owner, 22 Oct 2026: "leave google sing in for now also ask doddy is not wokring ifx it so it is avaiable for everyone using anthropic key"). Supersedes the OpenAI gateway for the app (ADR-113, `docs/architecture/ASSISTANT_GATEWAY.md`); defers Supabase and Google sign-in. <ul><li>**Why it didn't work:** the app sent typed questions to a separate gateway (`gateway/`, OpenAI, Vercel) that has no environment variables on any host (STATUS, "Known problems"), and Settings asked for a gateway address and an invite code that nobody had. The `backend/` server was already live, signs the iPhone in by itself (ADR-563) and holds `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (ADR-564). One server, one key, nothing to type.</li><li>**Server:** `POST /v1/assist` (`backend/src/assist.ts`). The request and reply are the gateway's wire contract (`schemas/fixtures/assist_*_v1.json`, now also checked against the server by `backend/test/assist.test.ts`), so the app's consent, note selection and citation check are unchanged. The saved notes the phone sends go to Claude as JSON data under aliases `n1…`; the answer's JSON schema lets it cite only those aliases (structured output, `output_config.format`, effort low, 1,500 tokens, 30 s), and the server maps them back to IDs, discarding any answer that cites something else. The model is told that notes and earlier turns are data, that it has no tools and can't claim to have saved anything, and to say when no note matched. `refusal` and `max_tokens` end an answer without showing it.</li><li>**Who may ask:** a Bearer session only (never the web cookie). 128 KB body. Strict input: the gateway's limits (question 2,000 characters, 6 earlier turns, 8 notes, 12,000 characters of notes, no unknown fields).</li><li>**Cost caps, before Claude runs and atomically** (`reserveUsage`): 50 questions per user and 500 for the whole server per UTC day (`DODDY_LIMIT_USER_DAILY_ASK`, `DODDY_LIMIT_SERVICE_DAILY_ASK`). At Sonnet 5.5 prices ($2 in, $10 out per million tokens) a question with eight notes and six earlier turns is about $0.04 at the very most (about 13,000 tokens in, 1,500 out) and usually under $0.01, so the worst day is about $20 and a typical one a few dollars. Past a cap the app says "Doddy's daily limit is reached".</li><li>**Every iPhone:** `DODDY_DEVICE_SIGNUP=open` (new, `render.yaml` sets it). The first iPhone still owns the server and gets the full API. Any other iPhone gets a guest session, a `ds1` token whose signed claim `scp: "ask"` makes `/v1/assist` the only route it can reach: the owner's accounts, web search, documents and the web sign-in code answer "not set up" for it (never 401, so the app doesn't loop). New guest sessions are limited to 20 an hour per network (an IPv6 /64) and 1,000 a day for the server. Guests are the same device user each time (ID derived from the device ID), so their daily cap follows them; a new install gets a new ID and a new cap, which the server-wide cap and the per-network limit bound.</li><li>**Logs** carry counts, tokens and timings, never the question, notes or answer. Nothing is stored.</li><li>**App:** `BackendAssistantClient` (DoddyCore) posts through the existing device session (`DeviceSession.ensure/renew`), renews once on 401, and maps the server's error codes. Settings loses the gateway address and invite-code rows; the status reads "On, through Doddy's server" or "Needs Doddy's server". Consent, the Ask footer and the privacy texts name Anthropic (Claude), not OpenAI; "What leaves this iPhone" lists "Your Doddy server and Anthropic (Ask Doddy)". The nine language tables were updated (drafts, not read by native speakers).</li><li>**Kept:** `GatewayAssistantClient`, `gateway/` and their tests, unused by the app, until the owner decides to delete them (audit decision 11).</li><li>**Google and Supabase sign-in** wait: the web still signs in with a code from the phone (ADR-600). Nothing was built for them.</li></ul> | **Superseded for the iPhone app on 23 Oct 2026 by ADR-585 and ADR-586 (Messages asks the messaging server, the owner's route B); the server route `POST /v1/assist` stays, the app no longer calls it.** Built; backend 327/327 and DoddyCore 1400/1400 tests pass here, the web end-to-end run still passes, and the app logic type-checks on Linux (`scripts/ci.sh --core-only`: PASS). **Not run against the real Anthropic API** (no key in this environment: the request shape is checked against a fake, and structured-output behaviour on `claude-sonnet-5-5` is unverified), not deployed, and the SwiftUI changes are uncompiled until the hosted Mac job. To turn it on: on the live `doddy` service set `DODDY_DEVICE_SIGNUP=open` (it was `first-device`) and deploy; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is already there for ADR-564. Then ask a question on a second iPhone. Owner: the Anthropic data-retention terms behind "How long Anthropic keeps them isn't something Doddy can verify" are not checked. |

## Logbook (2026-10-22)

| ADR | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 602 | **Logbook, a page in Doddy Web where each person sees how they use Doddy** (owner, 22 Oct 2026: "analytics for every people using Doddy… on a web app only… 2 different tabs… accessible for now… then… a paid option"; then, after a first copy on doddy.lol: "not this web app, the one connected with iPhone app", https://doddy-yaci.onrender.com). **Accepted by the owner 22 Oct ("go with recommendations"): all eight recommended decisions.** <ul><li>**Two tabs:** At a glance (free: the week as a till receipt, what Doddy was used for, when, notes saved, each day) and Closer look (free while in testing, part of a paid plan later: 7/30/90 days, every tool counted, weekday-by-hour, conversation lengths and endings, every yes and no, notes, lists, tasks handed over, languages, ways in, tables and CSV).</li><li>**Counts only:** the iPhone adds up its own day and, once the person turns Logbook on in the app, sends one allow-listed summary of whole numbers a day; the server refuses anything else. No note text, words, names, places, emails, events, tags or audio; private notes not even counted. Kept 90 days (decided); delete and download from the page and the app, never paywalled.</li><li>**No streaks, scores, comparisons or nudges** (ADR-306's rule, DOD-10). Couldn't confirm and Didn't work are their own counts (DOD-08).</li><li>**Where it lives:** Doddy Web (`web/src/pages/LogbookPage.tsx`, `web/src/logbook/`), signed in with the iPhone's one-time code (ADR-600), so nothing new for sign-in. Store: `backend/`'s Postgres (`logbook_state`, `logbook_days`: one row a day), written by the iPhone's own session only and read, downloaded and deleted through the web routes on the same origin. Taken back out of doddy.lol the same day.</li><li>**Decided 22 Oct:** the name Logbook / Dziennik; system-font fallbacks (no Google Fonts, no font files); 90 days; the translator counts sessions and minutes, never text; record each conversation's language and the way Doddy was opened (counts only); Closer look free while in testing, which plan decided at 1.0; deleted notes stay in past days' counts; the menu entry stays, marked Example, while the web app is only the owner's.</li><li>**Built 22 Oct:** the page (both tabs, Polish and English, light and dark) reading real days from the server, with off, empty, error, stale and unreadable-reply states, an example only on request, real download, CSV and delete; the server half (`backend/src/web/logbook.ts`: the allow-list contract; routes `PUT /v1/web/logbook/state`, `PUT /v1/web/logbook/days/:day`, `GET /v1/web/logbook`, `GET /v1/web/logbook/export`, `DELETE /v1/web/logbook`; 90-day sweep; fixtures in `contracts/web/`). `IPHONE_SENDS_LOGBOOK` (false) keeps the page from pointing at an app switch that doesn't exist.</li><li>**NOT BUILT:** the Doddy app's half (day counter, summary builder, upload, the switch and delete in Settings; no Swift toolchain in these sessions), so no real person has numbers; the paid gate. **Still the owner's:** which plan includes Closer look and from when; the database region and the legal review for the privacy policy.</li></ul> Plan: `docs/product/WEB_LOGBOOK.md`. First mockup: `design/web-logbook-v0.1/`. | Accepted; web and server built, iPhone half NOT BUILT. In `web/` (22 Oct): typecheck; `npm test` 76 pass; build; `npm run e2e` 14 pass (4 skipped: the screenshot specs, run separately and looked at), including Logbook against the real backend under its content security policy. `backend/` `npm test` 364 pass (37 new). **App counts, the switch, real data from a phone and the paid gate NOT BUILT; no real person has numbers on the page. Deployed 22 Oct 21:39 UTC (commit `d03562ae`, owner: fast-forward the live branch): start-up clean, routes answer; signed-in use on the live site not tried.** |
| 594 | **Doddy Web follows the website's design** (owner, 22 Oct 2026: "the website's design", for the web app connected to the iPhone app). <ul><li>**Look:** the cream canvas, the website's sticky header (wordmark, the sections in one row, Sign out; a Menu sheet under 864 px), paper cards with a soft lift, ink buttons with the orange underline, ruled notebook panels for Home and sign-in, Doddy's handwritten line (never an instruction, state or number). Class names the pages and tests rely on are kept.</li><li>**Colours:** from `CandidateTokens.swift`, the file the website's `tools/gen_site_tokens.py` reads, through `tools/gen_web_assets.py` (`--check` in CI). Before, `UploadedTokens.swift` (orange `#EC803F`); now the website's `#FF6A00`. Dark mode stays, with the same file's dark values (the website is light-only by the owner's request).</li><li>**Fonts:** Bricolage Grotesque and Caveat are the website's, from Google Fonts. Doddy Web's content security policy allows no outside fonts and no font files are bundled (`CLAUDE.md`), so the stacks name them first and fall back to Avenir Next, Segoe UI, Bradley Hand and Segoe Print. **Decided 22 Oct** (`WEB_LOGBOOK.md`, decision 2): keep the fallbacks. Matching the website exactly would need Google Fonts in the policy (sends visitors' IP addresses to Google) or self-hosting after a licence review.</li><li>**Poses:** five `WholeDoddy_*` poses copied by `gen_web_assets.py`; only the owner's pack.</li></ul> | Built. `web/` typecheck, 56 unit tests and 12 end-to-end tests pass; screenshots of every screen in light and dark, desktop and phone width, looked at (not seen on a real device). The security policy is unchanged. |
| 603 | **Doddy's server can keep a person's notes and conversations, as text, for a year, and chat with them** (owner, 22 Oct 2026: "I would like that data to be connected inside the app from the iPhone apps… you should have access to literally everything… your notes, your names… for a year, not like 90 days… the iMessage thing… connect with Haiku 5.5 or something"; chosen: "All text, 1 year" and "Server + web chat"). **This changes the promise that notes stay on the phone: it is now opt-in, and the page and the policy must say so.** <ul><li>**What:** notes and conversation transcripts as text, never audio, contacts or connected-app data. Two switches, set only by the iPhone, both off by default; off deletes that kind in the same transaction. Conversations kept 365 days; notes mirror the phone (a phone silent for a year loses them). Logbook (ADR-602) stays counts only and now keeps 1 year, not 90 days.</li><li>**How:** `backend/`'s own Postgres (`memory_state`, `memory_notes`, `memory_conversations`), AES-256-GCM with a **server-held key** `DODDY_MEMORY_KEY` (not end-to-end: the server can read it so it can search it, and Claude reads the best matches when asked), bound to user, kind and row; content-free logs; `private, no-store` replies. Per-person caps (1,500 conversations, 24 MiB), rate limits on search and download.</li><li>**Who can do what:** the iPhone's session writes; the browser reads, searches (words in the body), downloads, deletes and asks, and can never write or switch keeping on.</li><li>**Chat:** `POST /v1/web/chat`, Claude `DODDY_CHAT_MODEL` (default `claude-haiku-5-5`), notes and excerpts as JSON data under aliases, citations limited to what it was given, no tools, nothing stored, the same daily caps as Ask Doddy. `MemoryChat.answer(userId, …)` is channel-agnostic.</li><li>**Answers the open decisions 1 to 5 of `WEB_AND_SHARED_PLATFORM.md` section F:** opt-in sync; server-held key; backend's Postgres; text-only Claude through `backend/`; conversations 1 year.</li><li>**Web:** Memory (`/memory`: notes with search, conversations read in full, download, delete one conversation or everything) and Ask Doddy (`/ask`), Polish and English, marked Soon in the menu until `IPHONE_SYNCS_MEMORY` is flipped. The header's links collapse into the Menu sheet below 78rem and show their markers as dots above it.</li><li>**Independent security review before commit** found one blocking resource-exhaustion problem and several smaller ones; all fixed with tests (`docs/product/CLOUD_MEMORY.md`).</li><li>**iPhone half (23 Oct):** written. DoddyCore has the client, the rules (`WebMemoryModel`: the switch follows the server's answer, notes sent as the whole list, conversations one at a time with a retry list, private ones never sent) and the `DataFlow` rows, **tested on Linux (1,611 DoddyCore tests, 0 failures)**. The Settings switches, consent screen, delete button, the launch/foreground hooks and the call from `VoiceSessionController.keepLog` are **NOT COMPILED** (no Xcode here). Polish strings added.</li><li>**NOT BUILT:** iMessage and other text channels (designed only, `docs/architecture/TEXT_CHANNELS.md`: Apple Messages for Business is pending and needs a human handoff; SMS or WhatsApp need a verified link between a phone number and the person), key rotation, the Logbook half of the iPhone, any deploy. The privacy policy and store answers are drafted (`docs/legal/PRIVACY_POLICY_DRAFT.md` 5.13), not reviewed or published.</li><li>**Owner before launch:** set `DODDY_MEMORY_KEY` (never change it), confirm the database region, legal review, and whether notes should expire at a year.</li></ul> Plan and evidence: `docs/product/CLOUD_MEMORY.md`. | Built (server and web); iPhone rules tested, iPhone screens NOT COMPILED; not deployed. See STATUS for the test counts. |
| 604 | **The island follows what Doddy is doing, and its updates no longer queue** (owner, 23 Oct 2026: the Dynamic Island stutters; scan the code and make it fast). <ul><li>**Found:** the ElevenLabs `onAgentStateChange` callback never fired (SDK 3.3.1 reports state only when `agentStateConfiguration` is set), so the island, Home's waves and the talking pose stayed on Listening; island updates were chained one behind the other; Home's waves redrew at 120 Hz with nine offscreen opacity layers.</li><li>**Changed:** state events switched on; `LiveActivityPacer` (DoddyCore, 10 tests) and a single paced sender in `VoiceLiveActivity`; waves capped at 30 fps with colour alpha; unchanged phases skipped; opening scene capped at 60 fps; calendar/contacts reads in tool calls off the main actor; Doddy's clock at 60 Hz unless he really moves; cream edge only in dark mode; Home's finger-follow at 30 Hz. Detail and the suggestions not applied: `docs/implementation/ISLAND_SMOOTHNESS.md`.</li><li>**Live agent (owner, 24 Oct 2026: "do both"):** `agent_tool_request` added to the agent's client events, nothing else; version `agtvrsn_3501m5pk6ad9ed9v77hn049th27v`, rollback `agtvrsn_3801m5hyrqhee2ya90rh9hdat0m0` (`voice-agent/README.md`).</li></ul> | Linux: 1,672 DoddyCore tests, island checks and app-logic type-check pass. Hosted CI green on `3f623a8` ([run 40060080233](https://github.com/Bienos/doddy.2/actions/runs/40060080233): build, unit and UI tests); not seen on a device. |
| 605 | **Messages is drawn like the iPhone's Messages** (owner, 24 Oct 2026: it should look like iMessage and have the same functions; and say which model answers). <ul><li>**Model:** Claude Haiku 5.5 through Doddy's messaging server (ADR-586); unchanged.</li><li>**Changed (view only):** orange (Doddy's colour) and cream-grey bubbles with tails and runs, typing bubble, capsule composer with a round orange send button, a plus menu with the one real choice (use saved notes), press-and-hold Copy, tappable http(s) links, interactive keyboard dismiss. Facts and status: `docs/implementation/INFAKT_STATUS.md` (each fact tagged [V] read on inFakt's own pages, [S] third party, [U] not confirmed). <ul><li>**Draft and rules** (`Invoices/` in DoddyCore): `Sourced<T>` marks every field `saidByUser`, `userDefault` or `proposedByModel`; a field only the model suggested (the buyer's NIP, address, kind, a VAT rate, the payment method) counts as missing until the person says or confirms it. The NIP is checked (10 digits, weights 6-5-7-2-3-4-5-6-7, mod 11). Sums are integer grosze: a line's net is rounded half up, VAT is worked out per rate on the sum of nets. `issues()` names each missing, unconfirmed or wrong field (`contractor.nip`, `lines.2.vat_rate`, ...). The model never decides a NIP, an address or a VAT rate.</li><li>**Confirmation and send** (`InvoiceOperation`, `InvoiceSender`): the yes is bound to the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the exact invoice shown; changing the draft afterwards needs a new yes. `pendingExternalWrite` is saved before the call. "Issued" means a receipt that names this operation and carries the provider's id; anything else, and any timeout, is `unknownOutcome`, which is never success and never resent: the provider is asked, "none found" returns to the confirmation step, and a new send needs a new yes. `ConfirmedInvoice` can only be made by `InvoiceOperation`, so a provider cannot be handed an unconfirmed draft.</li><li>**The card** (`InvoiceCard`, `InvoiceCardView`): the wording, the sums and the problems are made in DoddyCore in Polish and English; the view only lays them out. It says "issued" only with a receipt and offers no Confirm button while `InFaktAPI.createVerified` is false.</li><li>**The key** (`AccountKey`, `AccountKeyManager`, `AccountKeyStore`, `InFaktSettingsView`): pasted into Settings › Invoices (inFakt), kept in the Keychain with `WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`, shown only as `••••` and its last four characters, never read back out to a screen. Printable ASCII with no space or line break only, so it can never become a second header. Save and Remove are reported only after the Keychain is asked again. Delete all and a fresh install remove it. The guard allows the Keychain only in the files that already hold a token or key, and a pasted-in key to be read only by the inFakt client, which does not exist.</li><li>**Not real, on purpose:** no request has ever been sent to inFakt, and the app has no inFakt client. The price units, the date fields, what the asynchronous create answers with and the KSeF address are [U]; a wrong guess would issue a wrong invoice on someone's books. `UnverifiedInFaktProvider` refuses to run (`InFaktAPI.createVerified = false`), the guard fails if the app names inFakt's address or key header without a client, and What leaves this iPhone says inFakt is not switched on. **Needs from the owner:** a free sandbox account and key (`konto.sandbox-infakt.pl`) so one real create call can settle the [U] rows. KSeF send is a separate confirmed operation, not built.</li><li>**Constitution evidence:** DOD-07 and ASK-01 (exact payload, approval bound to its digest), DOD-08 (a receipt or nothing; `unknown` is neither success nor retried), DOD-11 (a What leaves this iPhone row; the key's place), tool content as data (nothing reads an invoice from mail). **Claims to limit:** do not say "Doddy issues invoices" or that inFakt works; say the draft, the checks, the card and the key store exist and are tested on Linux.</li></ul> | DoddyCore `make test-core` (Swift 6.1.2, Linux): **1,896 tests, 0 failures** (78 new in this fork: `InvoiceTests` 25, `InvoiceCardTests` 10, `AccountKeyTests` 7, `AccountKeyManagerTests` 7, `DataFlowInvoiceAndParcelTests` 5, plus the parcel ones under 634); rules broken on purpose and caught, then restored (per file in the DoddyCore commit; in the later steps the Confirm gate, the read-back after a save and the look-up policy). `check_forbidden_capabilities.sh` PASS, and FAIL on a probe file with `import Security`, `AccountKeyStore.read`, inFakt's address and header, and a `print` in the key store; `typecheck_app_logic.sh` PASS (29 files); `build_strings_catalog.py --check` PASS (Polish lines added for the new screens). **BLOCKED / NOT RUN:** the SwiftUI screens, the Keychain on a device, `AccountKeyStore` and any real inFakt call (no Xcode, no sandbox key). `node tools/secrets/sync.mjs check` FAILS on `WATCHES_ENABLED` and `WATCHES_MAX_OPEN` in `server/messaging/.env.example`, as on the base (not touched here). |

| 642 | **Sport plan, what #137's engine did not have: the interview, a fixed stop for red-flag words, the pace of a weight goal, exercise cards, and one typed result, then a logbook, a weekly review and their screens** (owner, 24 Oct 2026: "dalszy plan sportowy" after the expert-modes research, ADR-630; built on `claude/sport-mode-health`). Chest, jaw or radiating pain, fainting, dizziness, unexplained breathlessness, palpitations and stroke-like signs give one fixed message in Polish or English that ends in 112, no plan, and no model can change it; a sharp injury or pregnancy gives its own fixed text and no plan; eating-disorder cues give a fixed text and switch off every number (ADR-620). Words are folded (accents, case); a negation within three words cancels a match ("nie mam bólu w klatce"); soreness after a workout is not chest pain unless something sudden is also said; "duszno" (stuffy air), "blood pressure" and "number" are not symptoms. It can only add a stop: finding no flag is never "all clear", the screening questions still come first.</li><li>**Weight goals** (`WeightGoalGuard.swift`): "schudnąć 10 kg w tydzień" is read as a pace; above 0.5 kg a week (ADR-620: about 500 kcal a day) the answer refuses, says the same loss takes about 20 weeks, and offers a movement plan; no goal at all under BMI 18.5, under 18, or with eating-disorder cues or pregnancy. No answer contains a calorie number.</li><li>**Exercise cards** (`ExerciseCard.swift`): 25 text-only cards, one for every exercise the plan engine can prescribe (a test sweeps the engine's menu) plus run/walk and easy run; Polish and English; cues, common mistakes, an easier version, a safety line that always says stop at sudden sharp pain and show it to a doctor or physiotherapist, WHO 2020 and ACSM 2026 (or the BJSM 2025 longest-run study for runs) cited with the day checked. Doddy's own words; no atlas, picture or video.</li><li>**One typed result** (`Expert/ExpertResult.swift`, `TrainingExpert.swift`): the research's contract (mode, answer, proposed actions, missing data, sources with `checked_at`, caveats, `memory_update_proposal` as null, risk level, `verified_external_action`). The orchestrator runs triage, then the weight goal, then the interview, then `PlanEngine`; Apple Health fills a gap only after the person's yes and says so; humor is off whenever the risk is above low. `verified_external_action` is false for everything here.</li><li>**Logbook, on this iPhone only, after the person's yes** (`Sport/TrainingLog.swift`, `SQLiteTrainingLogRepository.swift`, migration `v19_training_log`): a session has its day, the plan's week, a feeling (easy, okay, hard, **pain**), and either sets (exercise key, reps or seconds, load in grams, reps left) or run minutes. The rules sit in the value (`LoggedSet`, `LoggedSession`: a key is a short lowercase word, not free text; reps 0 to 200; load 0 to 500 kg; reps left 0 to 10; at most 40 sets) **and** as CHECK constraints in the tables, so no other path can write a row the value would refuse; a file read back goes through the same rules, a broken row is `corruptRecord` and is not shown. Nothing is written without consent, checked in the same transaction as the write; every write is read back inside its transaction and a difference rolls it all back (`verificationFailed`); a session more than a day ahead is refused. **Taking back the yes stops recording and does not delete**; **delete all** and delete one remove the rows, the sets and (`secure_delete`) the bytes; **export** (JSON, ISO dates, oldest first) works with or without consent. A correction replaces the entry whole. No note field, so no free text of a person's health is stored. Nothing here is sent anywhere.</li><li>**Weekly review** (`TrainingReview.swift`, `TrainingReviewText.swift`): code, not a model. It looks at the last 14 days and names **one** priority, in this order: pain in any session of the window (nothing is raised; see a doctor or physiotherapist; a mistaken entry can be corrected); a lighter step for an exercise whose sets stopped short of the range (`StrengthProgression`); the last two sessions both hard (the same as last time, nothing added); the first exercise ready for more, with **one variable** moving (+5 kg legs or +2.5 kg upper body and back to the bottom of the range, or 1 more rep, or a harder version at home, or a few seconds longer for a hold); else nothing changes. A set without "reps left" is counted as none, so it never raises anything and the text asks for the note. The totals of the last two sessions are compared only when the heaviest load was the same. A run is never raised (the plan already caps the long run at +10%). The words are fixed in Polish and English; none counts missed sessions, praises a streak or promises a result (a test scans every text for such words). `timedKeys` and the legs rule are copies of what the engine knows; a sweep test fails if the menu changes.</li><li>**Screens (stage 3, NOT COMPILED here):** Connections › Sport and health › Training log: the yes as a switch ("Turning this off stops saving. What is already saved stays until you delete it."), add a session, the review, the sessions with delete, an export file, delete my training log, the fixed safety note. The log is in its **own** file, `Application Support/Sport/training-log.sqlite`, in the plan's folder, which is left out of backups (5.1.3; ADR-625), not in the notes' file, which nothing excludes; the notes' "Delete all" does not reach it. The state is in `TrainingLogModel` (Foundation only, 11 tests with a fake store on Linux, type-checked), the view and the file wiring are not compiled. `check_training_plan_store.sh` was fixed (it stopped compiling when stage 1 added files). No voice tool, no live-agent change.</li><li>**Not decided, for the owner:** whether Settings' "Delete all memories" should also delete the log; the file-protection class of the log's file; after an eating-disorder cue the training mode gives the fixed text and no plan in that turn (the research only requires numbers off); whether it may still make a movement plan is the owner's call.</li></ul> | DoddyCore `make test-core` (Swift 6.1.2, Linux): **1,926 tests, 0 failures** (95 new in nine classes; stage 1, 49: `TrainingTriageTests` 12, `WeightGoalGuardTests` 6, `TrainingInterviewTests` 7, `ExerciseCardTests` 7, `ExpertResultTests` 5, `TrainingExpertTests` 12; stage 2, 46: `TrainingLogTests` 9, `TrainingReviewTests` 23, `SQLiteTrainingLogRepositoryTests` 14).
